\mathscr{L}^{E}\coloneqq\pi_{E!}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{E}})\cong 0_{E}^{!}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{E}})\in\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(S)

is
\otimes
-invertible.

The proof is in §
8.8.2
.

Theorem 8.2.4
.

For every
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
, there is a canonical isomorphism

(8.2.5)

(-)\otimes\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E})\to\FT_{\widehat{E}}\circ\FT_{E}(-)

of functors
\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(E)\to\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(E)
.

The proof is assembled at the beginning of §
8.8
.

Notation 8.2.5
.

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
.
Tensoring with
\mathscr{L}^{E}
defines an auto-equivalence

(-)\{E\}\coloneqq(-)\otimes\mathscr{L}^{E}\quad\text{of}~\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(S).

We denote its inverse by
(-)\{-E\}
.
We also write
(-)\{E\}\coloneqq(-)\otimes f^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E})
as an endofunctor of
\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(X)
where
f:X\to S
is any derived Artin stack over
S
with
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-action.
In this notation (
8.2.5
) reads:

(8.2.6)

(-)\{E\}\to\FT_{\widehat{E}}\FT_{E}(-).

Corollary 8.2.6
.

For every
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
, the functor
\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\bullet)\{-E\}
determines a canonical inverse to
\FT_{E}
.

8.2.6.
Base change

Proposition 8.2.7
.

For every morphism
f:S^{\prime}\to S
, denote by
f_{E}:E^{\prime}\to E
and
f_{\widehat{E}}:\widehat{E^{\prime}}\to\widehat{E}
its base changes.
Then there are canonical isomorphisms

(BC
∗
)

\displaystyle f_{\widehat{E}}^{*}\circ\FT_{E}

\displaystyle\cong{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}\circ f_{E}^{*},

(BC
∗
)

\displaystyle f_{\widehat{E}*}\circ{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}

\displaystyle\cong{\FT_{E}}\circ f_{E*},

(BC
!
)

\displaystyle f_{\widehat{E}!}\circ\FT_{E^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\cong{\FT_{E}}\circ f_{E!}

(BC
!
)

\displaystyle f_{\widehat{E}}^{!}\circ\FT_{E}

\displaystyle\cong{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}\circ f_{E}^{!}.

The proof is in §
8.5.2
and §
8.9
.

Lemma 8.2.8
.

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
.
For every morphism
f:S^{\prime}\to S
, we have a canonical isomorphism

(8.2.7)

f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E}\cong\mathscr{L}^{E^{\prime}}.

Moreover, if
f_{E}:E^{\prime}\to E
denotes the base change, there are canonical isomorphisms

(8.2.8)

\displaystyle f^{*}_{E}((-)\{E\})

\displaystyle\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\{E^{\prime}\},

(8.2.9)

\displaystyle f^{!}_{E}((-)\{E\})

\displaystyle\cong f_{E}^{!}(-)\{E^{\prime}\},

(8.2.10)

\displaystyle f_{E*}((-)\{E^{\prime}\})

\displaystyle\cong f_{E*}(-)\{E\},

(8.2.11)

\displaystyle f_{E!}((-)\{E^{\prime}\})

\displaystyle\cong f_{E!}(-)\{E\},

and similarly for
f_{\widehat{E}}:\widehat{E^{\prime}}\to\widehat{E}
.

The proof is in §
8.5.4
and §
8.9.6
.

8.2.7.
Functoriality

Proposition 8.2.9
.

For every morphism of derived vector bundles
\phi:E^{\prime}\to E
over
S
, there are canonical isomorphisms

(Fun
∗
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ex}^{*,\FT}

\displaystyle:\widehat{\phi}^{*}\circ{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}\to{\FT_{E}}\circ\phi_{!}

(Fun
∗
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ex}^{\FT,!}

\displaystyle:{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}\circ\phi^{!}\to\widehat{\phi}_{*}\circ{\FT_{E}}

(Fun
!
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ex}^{!,\FT}

\displaystyle:\widehat{\phi}^{!}\circ{\FT_{E^{\prime}}\{-E^{\prime}\}}\to\FT_{E}\{-E\}\circ\phi_{*}

(Fun
!
)

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ex}^{\FT,*}

\displaystyle:{\FT_{E^{\prime}}\{-E^{\prime}\}}\circ\phi^{*}\to\widehat{\phi}_{!}\circ{\FT_{E}\{-E\}}.

The proof is in §
8.5.3
and §
8.9
.

Example 8.2.10
.

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
.
Since the projection
\pi_{E}:E\to S
and zero section
0_{E}:S\to E
are dual to
0_{\widehat{E}}:S\to\widehat{E}
and
\pi_{\widehat{E}}:\widehat{E}\to S
, respectively, we get canonical isomorphisms

(8.2.12)

\displaystyle\FT_{0_{S}}\circ~\pi_{E!}

\displaystyle\cong 0_{\widehat{E}}^{*}\circ\FT_{E}

(8.2.13)

\displaystyle\FT_{E}\circ~0_{E!}

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{\widehat{E}}^{*}\circ\FT_{0_{S}}.

8.2.8.
Outline of proof: support and cosupport properties

We will see that the proof of involutivity (Theorem
8.2.4
) eventually boils down to what we call the “cosupport property” for the object
\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{E}})\in\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(E)
.

When
E
is of amplitude
\geqslant 0
, the object
\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{E}})
is supported on the zero section of
E
(which is a closed immersion):

Proposition 8.2.11

(Support property)
.

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
.
If
E
is of amplitude
\geqslant 0
, then we have:

(i)

There is a canonical isomorphism

(8.2.14)

{0}_{E}^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\mathbf{1}_{S}\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle.

(ii)

The canonical morphism

(8.2.15)

\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{unit}}{0_{E*}}{0_{E}^{*}}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\cong{0_{E*}}(\mathbf{1}_{S})\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle

is invertible.

In particular, there is a canonical isomorphism

(8.2.16)

\mathbf{1}_{S}\{E\}\cong 0_{E}^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\mathbf{1}_{S}\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle.

The proof is in §
8.7
. In general, the zero section
0_{E}
is not a closed immersion, so that
0_{E!}
does not agree with
0_{E*}
.
Nevertheless, the following dual version of Proposition
8.2.11
holds for
E
of arbitrary amplitude:

Proposition 8.2.12

(Cosupport property)
.

For every
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
, the object
\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{E}})\in\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(E)
lies in the essential image of the fully faithful functor
0_{E!}
.
More precisely, the canonical morphism

(8.2.17)

0_{E!}(\mathbf{1}_{S}\{E\})\cong 0_{E!}0_{E}^{!}(\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{E}}))\xrightarrow{\mathrm{counit}}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}_{\widehat{E}})

is invertible.

The proof is in §
8.8.1
.
Involutivity will then follow from:

Lemma 8.2.13
.

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
.
If
E
satisfies the cosupport property, then there is a canonical isomorphism

(-)\{E\}\to\FT_{\widehat{E}}\FT_{E}(-).

The proof is in §
8.6.2
.

8.2.9.
Identification of the twist

We do not know whether the twist
\mathscr{L}^{E}\cong\mathbf{1}_{S}\{E\}
can be identified with the Thom twist
\mathbf{1}_{S}\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle
in general.
If
E
admits a global presentation as a cochain complex of vector bundles, one can with some care build such an isomorphism from the vector bundle case.
To glue together these local isomorphisms (choosing presentations locally on
S
) we would need to show they are compatible up to coherent homotopy.
Assuming the existence of a suitable t-structure this question is reduced to the heart, where we just need to check a cocycle condition.
This line of argument leads to a proof of the following statement, by the same argument as in
[
FYZ22
, §A.3.5]
.
19
19

19

The gluing is quite subtle. This is due to the fact that if
E
is a vector bundle, the isomorphisms (
8.2.16
) for
E
and
\widehat{E}
only agree up to a sign.

Proposition 8.2.14
.

Suppose that the weave
\mathbf{D}
admits an orientation and a t-structure in which the unit is discrete, i.e.,
\mathbf{1}_{S}\in\mathbf{D}(S)^{\heartsuit}
for all derived Artin stacks
S
.
Then for every
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
there exists a canonical isomorphism

(8.2.18)

\mathscr{L}^{E}\cong\mathbf{1}_{S}\{E\}\cong\mathbf{1}_{S}\langle{-r}\rangle

where
r=\rk(E)
.

Remark 8.2.15
.

Proposition
8.2.14
applies to the weave
\mathbf{D}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({-};\mathbf{Q})
, by Lemma
3.4.1
.

8.3.
The contraction principle

The following
contraction principle
is well-known in the case of a separated morphism of schemes. In the context of
D
-modules it appears in
[
DG15
, Theorem C.5.3]
, and the proof works for an arbitrary topological weave.

Proposition 8.3.1

(Contraction principle)
.

Let
\pr:Y\to S
be a morphism of derived Artin stacks and
s:S\to Y
a section.
Suppose there is an
\mathbf{A}^{1}
-homotopy
Y\times\mathbf{A}^{1}\to Y
between
\mathrm{id}_{Y}
and
s\circ\pr
, so that the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y\times\mathbf{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s\circ\pr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}_{Y}}

commutes.
Then the canonical morphisms

\pr_{*}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{unit}}\pr_{*}s_{*}s^{*}\cong s^{*},\qquad s^{!}\cong\pr_{!}s_{!}s^{!}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{counit}}\pr_{!}

are invertible on
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant sheaves.

Corollary 8.3.2
.

For every derived Artin stack
Y
and every derived vector bundle
E
over
Y
, the natural transformations

\displaystyle\pi_{E*}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{unit}}\pi_{E*}0_{E*}0_{E}^{*}\cong 0_{E}^{*}

\displaystyle 0_{E}^{!}\cong\pi_{E!}0_{E!}0_{E}^{!}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{counit}}\pi_{E!}

are invertible on
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant sheaves.
In particular, the functors
\pi_{E}^{*}
,
\pi_{E}^{!}
,
0_{E*}
, and
0_{E!}
are all fully faithful on
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant sheaves.

Proof.

The first claim is a special case of Proposition
8.3.1
.
For every
\mathcal{K}\in\mathrm{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(Y)
there is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{K}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{E*}\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathcal{K})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0_{E}^{*}\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathcal{K})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{unit}_{\pi_{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{unit}_{0_{E}}}

where the vertical arrow is invertible by the first claim.
This shows that
\mathrm{unit}:\mathrm{id}\to\pi_{E*}\pi_{E}^{*}
is invertible on
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant sheaves.
Similarly, on
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant sheaves, the counit
\pi_{E!}\pi_{E}^{!}\to\mathrm{id}
is identified with the tautological isomorphism
0^{!}\pi^{!}\cong\mathrm{id}
; the counit
0_{E}^{*}0_{E*}\to\mathrm{id}
is identified with
\pi_{E*}0_{E*}\cong\mathrm{id}
; and the unit
\mathrm{id}\to 0_{E}^{!}0_{E!}
is identified with
\mathrm{id}\cong\pi_{E!}0_{E!}
.
∎

Corollary 8.3.3
.

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
.
For any Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0_{E}}

where
f
is smooth, the unit
\mathbf{1}_{Y}\to i^{!}i_{!}(\mathbf{1}_{Y})
is invertible.

Proof.

Apply
f_{0}^{*}
on the left to the isomorphism
\mathrm{unit}:\mathrm{id}\to 0_{E}^{!}0_{E!}
(Corollary
8.3.2
).
Under the isomorphisms
\mathrm{Ex}^{*}_{!}
and
\mathrm{Ex}^{*!}
(the latter since
f
is smooth), the result is identified with
\mathrm{unit}:f_{0}^{*}\to i^{!}i_{!}f_{0}^{*}
.
∎

8.4.
Computations on
{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
and
{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}\times{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}}

We lift some simple computations from
[
Lau03
]
(namely, Lemmas 1.4, 3.2, 3.3, and 3.4 of
op. cit.
) to the generality of topological weaves.

8.4.1.
The sheaf
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})

Let the notation be as follows:

(8.4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}

where
i
is the zero section.

We record some basic observations about the sheaf
j_{*}(\mathbf{1})\in\mathbf{D}^{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}(\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S})
, or more precisely

{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\in\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}})

where
{}^{\dagger}{j}:S={}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}\hookrightarrow{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
.

Proposition 8.4.1
.

(i)

Geometricity.

The sheaf
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})
is geometric.

(ii)

Base change.

For any morphism
f:S^{\prime}\to S
, let
j^{\prime}:S^{\prime}={}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{G}_{m,S^{\prime}}}\hookrightarrow{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S^{\prime}}}
denote the base change of
j
along
f^{\prime}:{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S^{\prime}}}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
.
Then the canonical morphism

\mathrm{Ex}^{*}_{*}:f^{\prime*}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\to j^{\prime}_{*}(\mathbf{1})

is invertible.

(iii)

Projection formula.

For every
\mathcal{K}\in\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}})
, the canonical morphism

\mathrm{Pr}^{*}_{*}:{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\otimes\mathcal{K}\to{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}^{*}(\mathcal{K})

is invertible.

(iv)

We have
{}^{\dagger}{\pr}_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\cong 0
in
\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{S})
.

(v)

There is a canonical isomorphism

{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\cong u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})[1]

in
\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}})
, where
j_{1}:\mathbf{A}^{1}\smallsetminus\{1\}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}
is the complement of the unit section and
u:\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}\twoheadrightarrow{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
is the quotient morphism.

Proof.

We consider the
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-scaling quotient of diagram (
8.4.1
), writing the resulting morphisms as
{}^{\dagger}{i}:{}^{\dagger}{S}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
, etc.
We have the localization triangles

(8.4.2)

{}^{\dagger}{j}_{!}\cong{}^{\dagger}{j}_{!}({}^{\dagger}{j}^{*})({}^{\dagger}{j}_{*})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{counit}}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{unit}}({}^{\dagger}{i}_{*})({}^{\dagger}{i}^{*}){}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}.

Applying
{}^{\dagger}{\pr}_{!}
yields

{}^{\dagger}{q}_{!}\to{}^{\dagger}{\pr}_{!}\ {}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{unit}}{}^{\dagger}{\pr}_{!}({}^{\dagger}{i}_{*})({}^{\dagger}{i}^{*}){}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\cong{}^{\dagger}{i}^{*}\ {}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}.

We have
{}^{\dagger}{\pr}_{!}\ {}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\cong{}^{\dagger}{i}^{!}\ {}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\cong 0

(iv)
by the Contraction Principle (Proposition
8.3.1
) and base change formula.
We deduce a canonical isomorphism

{}^{\dagger}{i}^{*}\ {}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\cong{}^{\dagger}{q}_{!}[1].

In particular, we can rewrite (
8.4.2
) as an exact triangle

{}^{\dagger}{j}_{!}\to{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\to{}^{\dagger}{i}_{*}\ {}^{\dagger}{q}_{!}[1].

Since
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{!}
,
{}^{\dagger}{i}_{*}={}^{\dagger}{i}_{!}
, and
{}^{\dagger}{q}_{!}
preserve geometric objects, it follows that
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}
preserves geometric objects.
Similarly, the base change and projection formulas for
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{!}
,
{}^{\dagger}{i}_{!}
, and
{}^{\dagger}{q}_{!}
yield the claimed base change and projection formulas for
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})
.
20
20

20

We omit verification of commutativity of some diagrams, expressing e.g. the compatibility of
\mathrm{Ex}^{*}_{*}:f^{\prime*}\ {}^{\dagger}{j_{*}}(\mathbf{1})\to j^{\prime}_{*}(\mathbf{1})
and
\mathrm{Ex}^{*}_{!}:j^{\prime}_{!}(\mathbf{1})\to f^{\prime*}\ {}^{\dagger}{j_{!}}(\mathbf{1})
.

For the final claim
(v)
, we begin by observing the canonical isomorphism

({}^{\dagger}{j}^{*})u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})\cong\mathbf{1}[-1]

using the base change formula
({}^{\dagger}{j}^{*})u_{!}\cong q_{!}j^{*}
for the Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S={}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces u}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{j}}

and the observation that

q_{!}j^{*}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})\cong\mathbf{1}[-1]

which is a straightforward computation using the base change formula and localization.

It will then suffice to show that the unit morphism

u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})\to{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}^{*}(u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1}))\cong{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})

is invertible.
By localization, this is equivalent to showing that
{}^{\dagger}{i}^{!}(u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1}))\cong 0
.
By the Contraction Principle (Proposition
8.3.1
), we have

{}^{\dagger}{i}^{!}(u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1}))\cong{}^{\dagger}{\pr}_{!}(u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1}))\cong{}^{\dagger}{q}_{!}(\pr_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1}))=0

since
\pr_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})=0\in\mathbf{D}(S)
by a straightforward localization argument.
∎

Corollary 8.4.2

(Preservation of geometricity)
.

Assume the weave
\mathbf{D}
is as in Theorem
8.2.1
(e.g.
\mathbf{D}=\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}(-;\mathbf{Q})
).
Then for every derived vector bundle
E\rightarrow S
over a derived Artin stack
S
, the functor
\FT_{E}
preserves geometricity.

Proof.

With notation as in (
8.2.3
), the functor
\FT_{E}
is the composite of the functors
\pr_{2}^{*}(-)
,
(-)\otimes\mathscr{P}_{E}[-1]
, and
\pr_{1!}(-)
. As discussed in §
8.2.3
, geometricity is preserved by
*
-pullbacks,
!
-pullbacks, and tensor product with geometric objects. Hence
\pr_{2}^{*}(-)
preserves geometricity on general grounds,
(-)\otimes\mathscr{P}_{E}[-1]
preserves geometricity because Proposition
8.4.1
(v) shows that
\mathscr{P}_{E}[-1]
is geometric, and
\pr_{1!}(-)
preserves geometricity because it can be identified with
!
-pullback to the zero section by Proposition
8.3.1
.
∎

8.4.2.
The square of
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})

We establish more technical properties of
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})
.

Lemma 8.4.3
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\boxtimes_{S}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\cong({}^{\dagger}{j}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{j})_{*}(\mathbf{1})\in\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}).

Proof.

By definition,

{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\boxtimes_{S}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\coloneqq\pr_{1}^{*}({}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1}))\otimes\pr_{2}^{*}({}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1}))

where
\pr_{1}
and
\pr_{2}
are the projections
{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
.
By smooth base change, we have
\pr_{1}^{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\cong j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})
, where
j_{1}={}^{\dagger}{j}\fibprod_{S}\mathrm{id}:S\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
and similarly for the second term.
By the projection formula (Proposition
8.4.1
),

j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})\otimes j_{2*}(\mathbf{1})\cong j_{1*}j_{1}^{*}j_{2*}(\mathbf{1}).

By smooth base change for the Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\fibprod_{S}S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}

where the diagonal composite is
{}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j}
, we have

j_{1*}j_{1}^{*}j_{2*}(\mathbf{1})\cong({}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j})_{*}(\mathbf{1}),

whence the claim.
∎

Consider the morphism

c=({}^{\dagger}{\pr_{1}},{}^{\dagger}{\pr_{2}}):{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}

induced by the projections
\mathbf{A}^{2}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}
(which are
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant), which exhibits
{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
as the quotient of
{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}
by the action
\lambda\cdot(x,y)=(x,\lambda\cdot y)
.
We have a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{G}_{m,S}\fibprod_{S}\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}\smallsetminus\{0\}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{1\}_{S}\fibprod_{S}\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{(\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}\smallsetminus\{0\}_{S})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{S}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i^{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{j^{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{i^{2}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{a}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{b}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{b}}

where the bottom row is the quotient of the middle one by the
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-action which scales the second coordinate (with weight
1
).
In the two left-hand columns, the horizontal rows are factorizations of
j\times j
,
{}^{\dagger}{j\times j}
, and
{}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j}
, respectively.
In the two right-hand columns, the horizontal rows are complementary open/closed immersions.

Let
\Delta\subset\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}
denote the diagonal,
i_{\Delta}:\Delta\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}
the inclusion, and
j_{\Delta}
the open complement of
i_{\Delta}
.

Lemma 8.4.4
.

There is a canonical isomorphism

({}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j})_{*}(\mathbf{1})\cong c_{!}({}^{\dagger}{j}_{\Delta*})(\mathbf{1})[1]\in\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\times_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}).

Remark 8.4.5
.

Let
e:\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}
denote the “difference” morphism, given informally by
(x,y)\mapsto x-y
.
By smooth base change for the square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{(\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}\smallsetminus\Delta)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{j}}

we can write
{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\cong{}^{\dagger}{j}_{\Delta,*}(\mathbf{1})
and hence also

(8.4.3)

({}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j})_{*}(\mathbf{1})\cong c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})[1].

Proof of Lemma
8.4.4
.

Set

\mathcal{K}\coloneqq c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta*}}(\mathbf{1})\in\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}).

We claim there are isomorphisms:

(a)

i_{b}^{!}(\mathcal{K})\cong 0
,

(b)

j_{b}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\cong{j}_{a*}(\mathbf{1})[-1]
.

By localization it will follow that the unit

\mathrm{unit}:\mathcal{K}\to j_{b*}j_{b}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\cong j_{b*}j_{a*}(\mathbf{1})[-1]\cong({}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j})_{*}(\mathbf{1})[-1]

is invertible, as claimed.

Proof of
(a)
.
Since
{}^{\dagger}{i^{2}}
is the zero section of the vector bundle
{}^{\dagger}{p^{2}}:{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}\to{}^{\dagger}{S}
(where
p^{2}:\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}\to S
is the projection), the Contraction Principle (Proposition
8.3.1
) yields natural isomorphisms isomorphism
({}^{\dagger}{i^{2}})^{!}\cong({}^{\dagger}{p_{0}^{2}})_{!}
and
i_{b}^{!}\cong({}^{\dagger}{p}\times{}^{\dagger}{p})_{!}
. Hence we have

\displaystyle i_{b}^{!}c_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta*}}

\displaystyle\cong({}^{\dagger}{p}\times{}^{\dagger}{p})_{!}c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta*}}\cong d_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{p_{0!}^{2}}\,{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta*}}\cong d_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{i^{2!}}\,{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta*}}

where
d
is the diagonal of
{}^{\dagger}{S}
as in the diagram above.
But
({}^{\dagger}{i^{2}})_{!}({}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta}})_{*}\cong 0
by base change (as
0\in\mathbf{A}^{2}
is contained in
\Delta
).

Proof of
(b)
.
Consider the diagram of Cartesian squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{G}_{m}}\smallsetminus\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{({\mathbf{G}_{m}}\times{\mathbf{G}_{m}}\smallsetminus\Delta)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times{\mathbf{G}_{m}}\smallsetminus\Delta)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{A}^{1}\smallsetminus\{1\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{(\mathbf{A}^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{({\mathbf{G}_{m}}\times{\mathbf{G}_{m}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times{\mathbf{G}_{m}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbf{A}^{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}\times{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}

where we omit the subscripts
S
for sanity of notation.
By base change we get

({}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j})^{*}c_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta*}}(\mathbf{1})\cong q_{!}j^{\prime}_{1*}(\mathbf{1})\cong\mathbf{1}[-1]

where the second isomorphism follows easily from localization.

Since
{}^{\dagger}{j}\times{}^{\dagger}{j}=j_{b}\circ j_{a}
this isomorphism gives by adjunction a morphism

j_{b}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\to j_{a*}(\mathbf{1})[-1]

in
\mathbf{D}(U)
which we claim is invertible.
Write
U
as the union of the two opens
[{}^{\dagger}{(\mathbf{A}^{1}\times{\mathbf{G}_{m}})}/{\mathbf{G}_{m}}]
and
[{}^{\dagger}{({\mathbf{G}_{m}}\times\mathbf{A}^{1})}/{\mathbf{G}_{m}}]
, where
[-/{\mathbf{G}_{m}}]
is the quotient by the scaling action on the second coordinate.
Over either open this morphism restricts to the isomorphism

u_{!}j_{1*}(\mathbf{1})\cong{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})[-1]

constructed in Proposition
8.4.1
(v).
∎

8.5.
Easy proofs

Here we spell out, for completeness, proofs which carry over essentially verbatim from the case of classical vector bundles.

8.5.1.
Zero bundle

We prove Proposition
8.2.2
. Recall that
i\colon 0_{S}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}
is the zero section and
j\colon\mathbf{G}_{m,S}\hookrightarrow\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}
is the complementary open embedding.

The evaluation map
\ev_{0_{S}}:[\widehat{0}_{S}/{\mathbf{G}_{m}}]\times[0_{S}/{\mathbf{G}_{m}}]\to[\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}/{\mathbf{G}_{m}}]
factors as the composite

B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}\times B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}\xrightarrow{m}B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}\xrightarrow{{}^{\dagger}{i}}[\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}/\mathbf{G}_{m,S}]

where the first map
m
is
(L,L^{\prime})\mapsto L\otimes L^{\prime}
.
By localization, one computes
{}^{\dagger}{i^{*}}{}^{\dagger}{j_{*}}(\mathbf{1})\cong{q_{!}}(\mathbf{1})[1]
, where
q:S\to B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}
.
Note that we have a Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}\times B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathbf{G}_{m,S}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\mathrm{id},o)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

where the left-hand vertical map is
L\mapsto(L,\widehat{L})
.
Thus the kernel of
\FT_{0}
is

\mathscr{P}_{0}=\ev_{0_{S}}^{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\cong m^{*}q_{!}(\mathbf{1})[1]\cong(\mathrm{id},o)_{!}(\mathbf{1})[1]

and
\FT_{0}
itself is given by

\FT_{0}(\mathcal{K})\cong\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes(\mathrm{id},o)_{!}(\mathbf{1}))[1][-1]\\
\cong\pr_{1!}(\mathrm{id},o)_{!}(\mathrm{id},o)^{*}\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\\
\cong o^{*}(\mathcal{K})

by the projection formula.

For the remaining isomorphisms, note that
o
is finite étale so that
o_{*}\cong o_{!}
and
o^{*}\cong o^{!}
.
Since
o
is an involution, we also have
o^{*}o^{*}\cong\mathrm{id}
, hence
o^{*}\cong o_{*}
and
o_{!}\cong o^{!}
. ∎

8.5.2.
Base change, part 1

Let the notation be as in Proposition
8.2.7
.

The base change property for
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})
(Proposition
8.4.1
) implies that there is a canonical isomorphism

(8.5.1)

f_{\widehat{E}\fibprod_{S}E}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{E})\cong\mathscr{P}_{E^{\prime}}

where
f_{\widehat{E}\fibprod_{S}E}:\widehat{E^{\prime}}\fibprod_{S^{\prime}}E^{\prime}\to\widehat{E}\fibprod_{S}E
is the base change of
f
.
The isomorphisms (
BC∗
) and (
BC!
) follow immediately using the base change and projection formulas.∎

The proofs of the remaining two isomorphisms will be done after proving involutivity.

8.5.3.
Functoriality, part 1

We prove the isomorphism (
Fun∗
) of Proposition
8.2.9
. We omit the base
S
from notation; all products are over
S
.

Let
\phi:E^{\prime}\to E
be a morphism of derived vector bundles.
We have the commutative square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times{\phi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\widehat{\phi}}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev_{E^{\prime}}}

whence a canonical isomorphism

(\mathrm{id}\times{\phi})^{*}\mathscr{P}_{E}^{*}\cong({\widehat{\phi}}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{E^{\prime}}).

We use the following commutative diagram, where all squares are Cartesian.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E^{\prime}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E^{\prime}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\phi}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\phi}\times\mathrm{id}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\phi}}

By the base change and projection formulas we have:

\displaystyle\FT_{E}{\phi_{!}}(\mathcal{K})[1]

\displaystyle\cong\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}({\phi_{!}}\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathscr{P}_{E})\cong\pr_{1!}((\mathrm{id}\times{\phi})_{!}(\pr_{2}^{*}\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathscr{P}_{E})

\displaystyle\cong\pr_{1!}(\mathrm{id}\times{\phi})_{!}(\pr_{2}^{*}\mathcal{K}\otimes(\mathrm{id}\times{\phi})^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{E}))\cong\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}\mathcal{K}\otimes({\widehat{\phi}}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{E^{\prime}})).

Similarly we have:

\displaystyle{\widehat{\phi}^{*}}\FT_{E^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K})[1]

\displaystyle\cong{\widehat{\phi}^{*}}\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathscr{P}_{E^{\prime}})\cong\pr_{1!}(\mathrm{id}\times{\phi})_{!}({\widehat{\phi}}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathscr{P}_{E^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\cong\pr_{1!}({\widehat{\phi}}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}(\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathscr{P}_{E^{\prime}})\cong\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes({\widehat{\phi}}\times\mathrm{id})^{*}\mathscr{P}_{E^{\prime}}).

Comparing these gives the desired isomorphism. ∎

8.5.4.
Base change for the twist, part 1

Given a morphism
f:S^{\prime}\to S
and
E^{\prime}\coloneqq E\fibprod_{S}S^{\prime}\in\operatorname{DVect}(S^{\prime})
, we show the isomorphisms

f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E}\cong\mathscr{L}^{E^{\prime}}

and

f^{*}_{E}((-)\{E\})\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\{E^{\prime}\},\qquad f_{E!}(-)\{E\}\cong f_{E!}((-)\{E^{\prime}\})

of Lemma
8.2.8
.
The remaining parts of these statements will be proven in §
8.9.6
.

By (
BC∗
) we have:

\displaystyle f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E}

\displaystyle=f^{*}\pi_{E!}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\pi_{E^{\prime}!}f_{\widehat{E}}^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\pi_{E^{\prime}!}\FT_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{1})=\mathscr{L}^{E^{\prime}}.

From
f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E}\cong\mathscr{L}^{E^{\prime}}
we now deduce

\displaystyle f_{E}^{*}((-)\{E\})

\displaystyle=f_{E}^{*}(-\otimes\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E}))\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\otimes f_{E}^{*}\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E})\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\otimes\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}f^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E})

\displaystyle\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\otimes\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E^{\prime}})=f_{E}^{*}(-)\{E^{\prime}\}.

Similarly, using the projection formula we have:

\displaystyle f_{E!}(-)\{E\}

\displaystyle=f_{E!}(-)\otimes\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E})\cong f_{E!}(-\otimes f_{E}^{*}\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E}))\cong f_{E!}(-\otimes\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}f^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E}))

\displaystyle\cong f_{E!}(-\otimes\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E^{\prime}}))=f_{E!}((-)\{E^{\prime}\}).

∎

8.6.
Proof of involutivity assuming cosupport

In this section we prove Lemma
8.2.13
.

8.6.1.
Kernel of the square

Consider the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}

A straightforward application of base change and projection formulas yields an identification

(8.6.1)

{\FT_{\widehat{E}}}\circ{\FT_{E}}(-)\cong\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}(-)\otimes\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime})[-2]

where

\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}\coloneqq\pr_{13!}(\pr_{12}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{\widehat{E}})\otimes\pr_{23}^{*}(\mathscr{P}_{E}))\in\mathbf{D}({}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}).

Consider the
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-action scaling both coordinates of
E\fibprod_{S}E
.
The two projections
\pr_{1},\pr_{2}:E\fibprod_{S}E\to E
are
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant.
We let
c:{}^{\dagger}{(E\fibprod_{S}E)}\to{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}
denote the induced morphism
({}^{\dagger}{\pr_{1}},{}^{\dagger}{\pr_{2}})
.
We denote by
e:E\fibprod_{S}E\to E
the “difference” morphism, given informally by
(x,y)\mapsto x-y
; this is also
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant.

Lemma 8.6.1
.

For any
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
, there is a canonical isomorphism

\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}\cong c_{!}({}^{\dagger}{e})^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})[2].

Proof.

Consider the morphism

\ev^{\prime\prime}:{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}

given on points by

\big((L,x:L\to E),(L^{\prime},\phi:L^{\prime}\to\widehat{E}),(L^{\prime\prime},y:L^{\prime\prime}\to E)\big)\\
\,\mapsto\,\big((L^{\prime}\otimes L,L^{\prime}\otimes L\xrightarrow{\phi\otimes x}\widehat{E}\fibprod_{S}E\xrightarrow{\ev}\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}),(L^{\prime}\otimes L^{\prime\prime},L^{\prime}\otimes L^{\prime\prime}\xrightarrow{\phi\otimes y}\widehat{E}\otimes E\xrightarrow{\ev}\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S})\big).

We have commutative squares

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{23}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{\prime\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}

This yields

(8.6.2)

\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}\cong\pr_{13!}\ev^{\prime\prime*}({}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\boxtimes_{S}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1}))\cong\pr_{13!}\ev^{\prime\prime*}c_{!}({}^{\dagger}{j}_{\Delta})_{*}(\mathbf{1})[1]

where the second isomorphism comes from Lemmas
8.4.3
and
8.4.4
.

Next observe that we have a commutative diagram

(8.6.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{S}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{{\mathbf{G}_{m}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{(E\fibprod_{S}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{(E\fibprod_{S}E)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{(\mathbf{A}^{2}\smallsetminus\Delta)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{j}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\Delta}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\pi_{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times{}^{\dagger}{e}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{e}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{j_{\Delta}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Delta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{13}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev^{\prime\prime}}

where the squares labeled by
\diamond
are derived Cartesian.
The notation is as follows:

•

The two projections
\pr_{1},\pr_{2}:E\fibprod_{S}E\to E
are
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-equivariant.
We let
c:{}^{\dagger}{(E\fibprod_{S}E)}\to{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}
denote the induced morphism
({}^{\dagger}{\pr_{1}},{}^{\dagger}{\pr_{2}})
.
Similarly for
c:{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{2}_{S}}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
.

•

\Delta
is the diagonal of
{}^{\dagger}{E}
and
{}^{\dagger}{\Delta}
is the quotient of the diagonal of
E
.

•

e:E\fibprod_{S}E\to E
is the “difference” morphism, given informally by
(x,y)\mapsto x-y
.
Similarly for
e:\mathbf{A}^{2}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}
.

From (
8.6.2
), applying proper and smooth base change isomorphisms to the Cartesian squares in (
8.6.3
) gives

(8.6.4)

\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}\cong\pr_{13!}\ev^{\prime\prime*}c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{\Delta*}{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}(\mathbf{1})[1]\cong c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}\pr_{2!}\ev_{E}^{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})[1].

Under the automorphism of
\widehat{{}^{\dagger}{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}
which swaps the factors, the morphism
\ev_{E}:\widehat{{}^{\dagger}{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}\to{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}
is identified with
\ev_{\widehat{E}}
and the projection
\pr_{2}:\widehat{{}^{\dagger}{E}}\fibprod_{S}{}^{\dagger}{E}\to{}^{\dagger}{E}
is identified with
\pr_{1}:{}^{\dagger}{E}\fibprod_{S}\widehat{{}^{\dagger}{E}}\to{}^{\dagger}{E}
.
Thus by definition we have an isomorphism

(8.6.5)

\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\pr_{2!}\ev_{E}^{*}{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})[-1].

Combining (
8.6.4
) and (
8.6.5
), we obtain an isomorphism

\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}\cong c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})[2]

as desired.

∎

8.6.2.
Proof of Lemma
8.2.13

Since
E
satisfies the cosupport property, we have the canonical isomorphism

0_{E!}(\mathcal{L}^{E})\coloneqq 0_{E!}0_{E}^{!}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\to\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1}).

Applying
c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}
yields a canonical isomorphism

c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}0_{E!}(\mathcal{L}^{E})[2]\to c_{!}{}^{\dagger}{e}^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})[2]\cong\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}.

By base change, using the diagram (
8.6.3
) again, we obtain a canonical isomorphism

\Delta_{!}{\pi_{E}^{*}}(\mathcal{L}^{E})[2]\to\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime}.

Finally, plugging this into (
8.6.1
) yields

\displaystyle\FT_{\widehat{E}}\FT_{E}(\mathcal{K})

\displaystyle\cong\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\mathscr{P}^{\prime\prime})[-2]\cong\pr_{1!}(\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes\Delta_{!}{\pi_{E}^{*}}(\mathcal{L}^{E}))

\displaystyle\text{(Projection formula) }\implies

\displaystyle\cong\pr_{1!}(\Delta_{!}(\Delta^{*}\pr_{2}^{*}(\mathcal{K})\otimes{\pi_{E}^{*}}(\mathcal{L}^{E})))\cong\mathcal{K}\otimes{\pi_{E}^{*}}(\mathcal{L}^{E}).\qed

8.7.
Co/support and involutivity for
E\geqslant 0

In this section we fix
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
of amplitude
\leqslant 0
.
We prove the support property Proposition
8.2.11
for
E
(which implies the cosupport property too) and deduce the optimal form of involutivity in this case.

8.7.1.
Restriction to zero

We first compute the inverse image along
{}^{\dagger}{0}_{E}:{}^{\dagger}{S}\to{}^{\dagger}{E}
:

{}^{\dagger}{0}_{E}^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\cong\FT_{0_{S}}({}^{\dagger}{\pi}_{\widehat{E}!}(\mathbf{1}))\cong\FT_{0_{S}}(\mathbf{1}\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle)\cong\mathbf{1}\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle,

using functoriality as well as the isomorphisms
{}^{\dagger}{\pi}_{\widehat{E}}^{!}\cong{}^{\dagger}{\pi}_{\widehat{E}}^{*}\langle{\widehat{E}}\rangle
(Poincaré duality) and
({}^{\dagger}{\pi}_{\widehat{E}})_{!}({}^{\dagger}{\pi}_{\widehat{E}})^{!}\cong\mathrm{id}
(homotopy invariance) for the morphism
{}^{\dagger}{\pi}_{\widehat{E}}:{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\to{}^{\dagger}{S}
(smooth because
\widehat{E}
is of amplitude
\leqslant 0
).

8.7.2.
Support and cosupport properties

Since
E
is of amplitude
\geqslant 0
, the zero-section
0_{E}
is a closed immersion and
0_{E*}=0_{E!}
.
By localization, invertibility of either (
8.2.15
) or (
8.2.17
) is equivalent to the assertion that
\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})
is supported on the image of
{}^{\dagger}{0}_{E}:{}^{\dagger}{S}\to{}^{\dagger}{E}
.

It will suffice to show that for every residue field
v:\Spec(\kappa)\to{}^{\dagger}{E}
that factors through the complement of
0_{E}
,
v^{*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})
vanishes.
Without loss of generality, we may replace
S
by
\Spec(\kappa)
and show that for every nowhere zero section
s
of
E\to S
, the inverse image along
s^{\prime}:S\xrightarrow{s}E\twoheadrightarrow{}^{\dagger}{E}
vanishes.

We have the following commutative diagram:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\times{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces a_{\widehat{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{id}\times s^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}

where the left-hand square is Cartesian.
Here
{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s}}
is the
{\mathbf{G}_{m}}
-quotient of the “evaluation at
s
” morphism
\ev_{s}:\widehat{E}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}
, and
a_{\widehat{E}}
is the projection.
Hence we have

(8.7.1)

s^{\prime*}\pr_{2!}\ev^{*}\cong a_{\widehat{E}!}(\mathrm{id}\times s^{\prime})^{*}\ev^{*}\cong a_{\widehat{E}!}{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s}^{*}}.

Since
s
is nowhere zero,
\ev_{s}
is surjective and its fibre
F=\Fib(\ev_{s}:\widehat{E}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S})
is of amplitude
\leqslant 0
.
Since
S
is affine, it follows that
\ev_{s}
admits a section, hence can be identified with the projection
F\fibprod_{S}\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}\to\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}
.
Using Poincaré duality and homotopy invariance for the latter we have

(8.7.2)

a_{\widehat{E}!}\,{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s}^{*}}\cong a_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s!}}{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s}^{*}}\cong a_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s!}}{}^{\dagger}{\ev_{s}^{!}}\langle{-F}\rangle\cong a_{!}\langle{-F}\rangle

where
a:{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\to S
is the projection.
Combining with (
8.7.1
) we deduce

(8.7.3)

s^{\prime*}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})\coloneqq s^{\prime*}\pr_{2!}\ev^{*}({}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1}))\cong a_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{1})\langle{-F}\rangle.

Since
a
factors through
{}^{\dagger}{p}:{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}_{S}}\to{}^{\dagger}{S}
, and
{}^{\dagger}{p}_{!}\,{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}\cong 0
(Proposition
8.4.1
), (
8.7.3
) vanishes, as desired. ∎

8.7.3.
Proof of involutivity

At this point, combining Proposition
8.2.11
with Lemma
8.2.13
yields the following form of involutivity:

Corollary 8.7.1

(Involutivity)
.

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
be of amplitude
\geqslant 0
.
Then there is a canonical isomorphism

(-)\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle\to\FT_{\widehat{E}}\FT_{E}(-).

Proof.

By Lemma
8.2.13
, the cosupport property for
\widehat{E}
yields the canonical isomorphism

(-)\otimes\pi_{E}^{*}(\mathscr{L}^{E})\to\FT_{\widehat{E}}\FT_{E}(-).

By Proposition
8.2.11
(ii)
, we have the canonical isomorphism

(8.7.4)

\mathscr{L}^{E}\cong\pi_{E!}0_{E*}(\mathbf{1})\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle\cong\pi_{E!}0_{E!}(\mathbf{1})\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle\cong\mathbf{1}_{S}\langle{-\widehat{E}}\rangle.

∎

8.8.
Cosupport and involutivity for general
E

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
be an arbitrary derived vector bundle.
We will now prove Proposition
8.2.12
, which implies involutivity (Theorem
8.2.4
) by Lemma
8.2.13
.
We will also show that the twist
\mathscr{L}^{E}=\mathbf{1}\{E\}
is
\otimes
-invertible in general (Lemma
8.2.3
).

8.8.1.
Proof of Proposition
8.2.12

Let
E\in\operatorname{DVect}(S)
and let us show that the canonical morphism

(8.8.1)

\mathrm{counit}:0_{\widehat{E}!}0_{\widehat{E}}^{!}\FT_{E}(\mathbf{1}_{E})\to\FT_{E}(\mathbf{1}_{E})

is invertible.
Equivalently,
\FT_{E}(\mathbf{1}_{E})
lies in the essential image of the fully faithful functor
0_{\widehat{E}!}
(Corollary

).

Let

be a smooth surjection and adopt the notation of Proposition

.
Applying

on the left to the morphism (

) yields, by the base change formula

, the exchange isomorphism

, and (

), a canonical morphism

0_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}!}0_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}^{!}\FT_{E^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(\mathbf{1}_{E^{\prime}})\to\FT_{E^{\prime}/S^{\prime}}(\mathbf{1}_{E^{\prime}})

which one easily checks is identified with the counit.
Thus the claim is local on
S
and we may assume that
S
is affine.
In particular, by choosing a global presentation
\mathcal{E}
as in §
2.4
, we may write
E
as the fibre of a morphism
d:E^{-}\to E^{+}
where
E^{-}
is of amplitude
\leqslant 0
and
E^{+}
is of amplitude
\geqslant 0
.
21
21

21

namely, if
(\cdots\to\mathcal{E}^{-1}\to\mathcal{E}^{0}\to\mathcal{E}^{1}\to\cdots)
is a global presentation for
\mathcal{E}
, take
E^{-}=\Tot_{S}(\mathcal{E}^{\leq 0})
and
E^{+}=\Tot_{S}(\mathcal{E}^{\geq 1}[1])
to be the brutal truncations

We have the Cartesian squares

(8.8.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{+},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0_{E^{+}}}

) and its dual

We may thus check (

) is invertible after applying

this reduces to show that the morphism

\mathrm{counit}:\widehat{d}_{!}\widehat{d}^{!}\FT_{E^{-}}(i_{!}\mathbf{1}_{E})\to\FT_{E^{-}}(i_{!}\mathbf{1}_{E})

We claim that
\FT_{E^{-}}(i_{!}\mathbf{1}_{E})

\widehat{d}_{!}
.
Since

Using

Using involutivity for

8.8.2.
Proof of Lemma
8.2.3

We prove that
\mathscr{L}^{E}\coloneqq\pi_{E!}\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\mathbf{1})
is
\otimes
-invertible.
This is equivalent to the assertion that the evaluation morphism

(8.8.7)

\ev:\mathscr{L}^{E}\otimes\underline{\smash{\Hom}}(\mathscr{L}^{E},\mathbf{1}_{S})\to\mathbf{1}_{S}

is invertible.
If
f:S^{\prime}\to S
is a smooth morphism, then we have the canonical isomorphism

f^{*}\underline{\smash{\Hom}}(\mathscr{L}^{E},\mathbf{1}_{S})\cong\underline{\smash{\Hom}}(f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E},f^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S^{\prime}})

under which the
*
-pullback of (
8.8.7
) is identified with

f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E}\otimes\underline{\smash{\Hom}}(f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E},f^{*}\mathbf{1}_{S^{\prime}})\to\mathbf{1}_{S^{\prime}}.

Let

and let

be the base change of
f
.
By §

we have
f^{*}\mathscr{L}^{E}\cong\mathscr{L}^{E^{\prime}}
.
This shows that the question of invertibility of (

) is local on
S
.
In particular, we may assume that
E
admits a presentation so that there are Cartesian squares

(8.8.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{-}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E^{+},}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces d}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0_{E^{+}}}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{E^{+}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{E^{-}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces S}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{\widehat{E^{+}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{i}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 0_{\widehat{E}}}

where

and

is of amplitude

, as above in (

).
By symmetry, we may as well show the claim for

is

-invertible.
After
*
-pullback along the smooth surjection
\pi_{\widehat{E^{+}}}:\widehat{E^{+}}\to S
, we have:

(Corollary

)

\displaystyle\pi_{\widehat{E^{+}}}^{*}\mathscr{L}^{\widehat{E}}

\displaystyle\cong\pi_{\widehat{E^{+}}}^{*}0_{\widehat{E}}^{!}\FT_{E}(\mathbf{1})

(
\mathrm{Ex}^{*!}
)

\displaystyle\cong\widehat{d}^{!}\widehat{i}^{*}\FT_{E}(\mathbf{1})

(
Fun∗
)

\displaystyle\cong\widehat{d}^{!}\FT_{E^{-}}(i_{!}\mathbf{1})

(

)

which is evidently

is from the isomorphism

(
