).

8.9.
Base change and functoriality, part 2

Using involutivity, we conclude the proofs of Propositions
8.2.7
and
8.2.9
.
Specifically, we will use the fact that the functor

\FT_{\widehat{E}}(\bullet)\{-E\}

is inverse to
\FT_{E}
(Corollary
8.2.6
).

8.9.1.
Proof of (
BC∗
)

Let the notation be as in Proposition
8.2.7
.
Recall the isomorphism

f^{*}_{E}((-)\{E\})\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\{E^{\prime}\}

of Lemma
8.2.8
, proven in §
8.5.4
.
Passing to right adjoints, we have:

(8.9.1)

f_{E*}(-)\{-E\}\cong f_{E*}((-)\{-E^{\prime}\})

Recall also the
*
-base change natural isomorphism (
BC∗
)

f_{\widehat{E}}^{*}\FT_{E}\cong\FT_{E^{\prime}}f_{E}^{*}

proven in §
8.5.2
.
Passing to right adjoints yields

f_{E*}(\FT_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}(-)\{-E^{\prime}\})\cong\FT_{\widehat{E}}(f_{\widehat{E}*}(-))\{-E\}.

Pulling out the twist using (
8.9.1
), we deduce

f_{E*}(\FT_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}(-))\cong\FT_{\widehat{E}}(f_{\widehat{E}*}(-)).

Equivalently, replacing
E
by
\widehat{E}
gives the natural isomorphism

(8.9.2)

f_{\widehat{E}*}(\FT_{E^{\prime}}(-))\cong\FT_{E}(f_{E*}(-)).

8.9.2.
Proof of (
BC!
)

This follows from (
BC!
) exactly as above.

8.9.3.
Proof of (
Fun!
)

Passing to right adjoints from (
Fun∗
)

\mathrm{Ex}^{*,\FT}:\widehat{\phi}^{*}\circ{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\FT_{E}}\circ\phi_{!}

yields the canonical isomorphism

(8.9.3)

\phi^{!}\circ{\FT_{\widehat{E}}\{-\widehat{E}\}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\FT_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}\{-\widehat{E^{\prime}}\}}\circ\widehat{\phi}_{*}.

Equivalently, applying this to
\widehat{\phi}
in place of
\phi
we get the canonical isomorphism

(8.9.4)

\mathrm{Ex}^{!,\FT}:\widehat{\phi}^{!}\circ{\FT_{E^{\prime}}\{-E^{\prime}\}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\FT_{E}\{-E\}}\circ\phi_{*}.

∎

8.9.4.
Proof of (
Fun∗
)

Begin with the isomorphism (
8.9.3
) above.
Applying
\FT_{E^{\prime}}
on the left and
\FT_{E}
on the right, then applying the natural isomorphism of Theorem
8.2.4
to
\FT_{E^{\prime}}\circ\FT_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}
and
(-1)^{\rank E}
times the natural isomorphism of Theorem
8.2.4
to
\FT_{\widehat{E}}\circ\FT_{E}
(cf.
[
FYZ22
, §A.2.6]
), and then untwisting, we obtain the natural isomorphism

\mathrm{Ex}^{\FT,!}:{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}\circ\phi^{!}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\phi}_{*}\circ{\FT_{E}}.

∎

8.9.5.
Proof of (
Fun!
)

Begin with the natural isomorphism (
Fun∗
):

\mathrm{Ex}^{*,\FT}:\widehat{\phi}^{*}\circ{\FT_{E^{\prime}}}\xrightarrow{\sim}{\FT_{E}}\circ\phi_{!}.

Applying
\FT_{E^{\prime}}\{-E\}
on the left and
\FT_{E}\{-E\}
on the right then applying the natural isomorphism of Theorem
8.2.4
to
\FT_{E^{\prime}}\circ\FT_{\widehat{E^{\prime}}}
and
(-1)^{\rank E}
times the natural isomorphism of Theorem
8.2.4
to
\FT_{\widehat{E}}\circ\FT_{E}
, and then untwisting, we obtain the natural isomorphism:

\mathrm{Ex}^{\FT,*}:{\FT_{E^{\prime}}\{-E^{\prime}\}}\circ\phi^{*}\xrightarrow{\sim}\widehat{\phi}_{!}\circ{\FT_{E}\{-E\}}.

∎

8.9.6.
Proof of Lemma
8.2.8

Let
f:S^{\prime}\to S
be a morphism and adopt the notation of Proposition
8.2.7
.
We prove the remaining isomorphisms of Lemma
8.2.8
.
We already have the isomorphisms

(8.9.5)

f^{*}_{E}((-)\{E\})\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\{E^{\prime}\},\qquad f_{E!}(-)\{E\}\cong f_{E!}((-)\{E^{\prime}\})

by §
8.5.4
.

8.9.7.
*
-Push

We wish to produce a natural isomorphism

f_{E*}(-)\{E\}\cong f_{E*}((-)\{E^{\prime}\})

which is equivalent by passage to left adjoints to a natural isomoprhism

f_{E}^{*}((-)\{-E\})\cong f_{E}^{*}(-)\{-E^{\prime}\}.

Twisting by
\{E^{\prime}\}
on both sides, this is equivalent to a natural isomorphism

f_{E}^{*}((-)\{-E\})\{E^{\prime}\}\cong f_{E}^{*}(-),

which we have from (
8.9.5
).∎

8.9.8.
!
-Pull

This follows from the
!
-push version by the same argument as above. ∎

9.
Fourier analysis for motives

We now specialize the discussion again to the weave
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({-};\mathbf{Q})
.
We will refer to the homogeneous Fourier transform in this context as the “motivic homogeneous Fourier transform”. In this section we analyze the interaction between the motivic Fourier transform and cohomological correspondences, and the motivic sheaf-cycle correspondence. In §
9.3
we recall the notion of cohomological co-correspondences. §
9.1
and §
9.2
play a small technical role. In §
9.4.1
and §
9.4.2
we study the motivic Fourier transform of cohomological correspondences and their compatibility with pushforwards and pullbacks. Finally in §
9.5
we define a homogeneous version of the
arithmetic Fourier transform
from
[
FYZ22
, §9]
, and establish its compatibility with the motivic Fourier transform under the sheaf-cycle correspondence.

9.1.
The renormalized homogeneous Fourier transform

9.1.1.

Let
S
be a derived Artin stack locally of finite type over a field.
For a derived vector bundle
E\rightarrow S
, the
homogeneous motivic Fourier transform
is the functor

\FT_{E}:\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E}};\mathbf{Q})\to\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}};\mathbf{Q})

given by the construction of §
8.2.2
in the case of the weave
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({-};\mathbf{Q})
.

9.1.2.

The
renormalized
homogeneous motivic Fourier transform

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E}};\mathbf{Q})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}};\mathbf{Q})

is defined as

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E}(-)\coloneqq\FT_{E}(-)[\rk(E)].

As all sheaf-theoretic operations are compatible with shifts, the results of §
8
all have straightforward reformulations in terms of
\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}
.

Remark 9.1.1
.

The renormalization is designed to match the conventions for the
\ell
-adic Fourier transform in
[
Lau87
,
FYZ22
]
.
Indeed, in the
\ell
-adic context the “average” of the Artin–Schreier sheaf
\mathcal{L}_{\psi}
is isomorphic to
{}^{\dagger}{j}_{*}(\mathbf{Q})[-1]
where
{}^{\dagger}{j}\colon{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{G}_{m}}\rightarrow{}^{\dagger}{\mathbf{A}^{1}}
by
[
Lau03
, Lemme 2.3]
(notation as in
8.1.2
).
Hence
\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E}
is compatible with the derived
\ell
-adic Fourier transform of
[
FYZ22
]
under
\ell
-adic realization.
On the other hand, one can show that
\FT_{E}
(non-renormalized) is compatible with the Fourier–Sato transform of
[
KS90
,
KKa
]
under Betti realization (see
[
KKb
]
).

Remark 9.1.2
.

The renormalization is arguably less natural from the perspective of weights, but more natural from the perspective of perverse sheaves.

9.2.
Further functoriality properties

We formulate some functoriality results that we can prove under globally presented assumptions (which are probably unnecessary for the statements to be true).
We recall the notion of global presentations of derived vector bundles from §
2.4
.

9.2.1.
Base change

Consider a Cartesian square of globally presented derived vector bundles over
S
, along with the dual Cartesian square:

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}

and

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{C}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{D}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{B}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{g}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{g}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}^{\prime}}

Then the base change formula gives natural isomorphisms

(9.2.1)

g^{*}f_{!}\cong(f^{\prime})_{!}(g^{\prime})^{*}\quad\text{and}\quad\widehat{g}_{!}\widehat{f}^{*}\cong(\widehat{f}^{\prime})^{*}\widehat{g}^{\prime}_{!}

Let
d\coloneqq d(f)
,
\delta\coloneqq d(g)
. According to §
8.2.7
, there are natural isomorphisms

(9.2.2)

\widehat{g}_{!}\widehat{f}^{*}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{A}\cong\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{D}g^{*}f_{!}[d+\delta](\delta)\quad\text{and}\quad(\widehat{f}^{\prime})^{*}\widehat{g}^{\prime}_{!}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{A}\cong\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{D}f^{\prime}_{!}(g^{\prime})^{*}[d+\delta](\delta).

Proposition 9.2.1
.

Assume that
f
and
g
are globally presented (in particular,
A,C,D
are globally presented). Then there is a commutative diagram of functors
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({A};\mathbf{Q})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({\widehat{D}};\mathbf{Q})

(9.2.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{g}_{!}\widehat{f}^{*}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{D}g^{*}f_{!}[d+\delta](\delta)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{f}^{\prime})^{*}\widehat{g}^{\prime}_{!}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{A}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{D}f^{\prime}_{!}(g^{\prime})^{*}[d+\delta](\delta)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}

where the identifications are as in (
9.2.1
) and (
9.2.2
).

Proof.

The proof of
[
FYZ22
, Proposition 6.6.3]
works verbatim.
∎

9.2.2.
Gysin vs. forgetting supports

Recall from
[
FYZ22
, §6.4]
that a map
f\colon E^{\prime}\rightarrow E
of derived vector bundles over
S
is quasi-smooth if and only if the dual map
\widehat{f}\colon\widehat{E}\rightarrow\widehat{E^{\prime}}
is separated.
In this case, we have a Gysin natural transformation (see §
3.6
)

\mathrm{gys}_{f}\colon f^{*}\rightarrow f^{!}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(f)\rangle}

and a “forget supports” natural transformation

\mathrm{fsupp}_{\widehat{f}}\colon\widehat{f}_{!}\rightarrow\widehat{f}_{*}.

Proposition 9.2.2
.

Let
f\colon E^{\prime}\rightarrow E
be a globally presented quasi-smooth map of derived vector bundles and let
\widehat{f}\colon\widehat{E}\rightarrow\widehat{E}^{\prime}
be the dual map to
f\colon E^{\prime}\rightarrow E
. Then there is a commutative diagram of functors
\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E}};\mathbf{Q})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}^{\prime}}};\mathbf{Q})

(9.2.4)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}_{!}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}_{*}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E^{\prime}}f^{*}[d(f)](d(f))}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E^{\prime}}f^{!}[-d(f)]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\operatorname{fsupp}_{\widehat{f}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sim}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{gys}_{f}}

Proof.

The proof of
[
FYZ22
, Proposition 6.4.2]
works verbatim.
∎

9.3.
Cohomological co-correspondences

A
co-correspondence
between derived Artin stacks
A_{0}
and
A_{1}
is a diagram

(9.3.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\prime}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c^{\prime}_{0}}

We define a
cohomological co-correspondence
from
\mathcal{K}_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({A_{0}};\mathbf{Q})
to
\mathcal{K}_{1}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({A_{1}};\mathbf{Q})
to be an element of
\Hom_{C^{\prime}}(c^{\prime}_{1!}\mathcal{K}_{0},c^{\prime}_{0*}\mathcal{K}_{1})
. Let

(9.3.2)

\mathrm{CoCorr}_{C^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\coloneqq\Hom_{C^{\prime}}(c^{\prime}_{1!}\mathcal{K}_{0},c^{\prime}_{0*}\mathcal{K}_{1}).

9.3.1.
Correspondences versus co-correspondences

To see the relation between cohomological correspondences and co-correspondences, suppose we have a Cartesian square

(9.3.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces A_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces c_{0}^{\prime}}

Then for
\mathcal{K}_{0}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({A_{0}};\mathbf{Q})
and
\mathcal{K}_{1}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({A_{1}};\mathbf{Q})
, there is a canonical isomorphism of vector spaces

(9.3.4)

\gamma_{C}:\mathrm{Corr}_{C^{\flat}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{CoCorr}_{C^{\sharp}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})

given by the composition below, where the isomorphisms come from adjunctions and proper base change:

\Hom_{C^{\flat}}(c_{0}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{0},c_{1}^{!}\mathcal{K}_{1})\cong\Hom_{A_{1}}(c_{1!}c_{0}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\cong\Hom_{A_{1}}((c_{0}^{\prime})^{*}c^{\prime}_{1!}\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\cong\Hom_{C^{\sharp}}(c^{\prime}_{1!}\mathcal{K}_{0},c^{\prime}_{0*}\mathcal{K}_{1}).

Note that a correspondence of
derived vector bundles
(over some base
S
) can always be completed to a Cartesian square of the form (
9.3.3
) by taking
C^{\sharp}
to be the pushout in the
\infty
-category of derived vector bundles over
B
.

9.4.
Fourier transform of cohomological correspondences

In §
9.3
we defined the notion of cohomological co-correspondence. These arise naturally as the Fourier transforms of cohomological correspondences, as we now explain.

9.4.1.

Suppose we are given a map of correspondences of derived Artin stacks

(9.4.1)

\textstyle{E_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{C^{\flat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{0}}
\textstyle{C_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{0}}
\scriptstyle{h_{1}}
\textstyle{S_{1}}

where
E_{0},C^{\flat}
and
E_{1}
are derived vector bundles on
S_{0},C_{S}
and
S_{1}
respectively. Assume the maps
p_{0}
and
p_{1}
are linear.

Let
\widetilde{E}_{0}
and
\widetilde{E}_{1}
be the pullbacks of
E_{0}
and
E_{1}
to
C_{S}
via
h_{i}
. We can canonically extend the correspondence
E_{0}\xleftarrow{p_{0}}C^{\flat}\xrightarrow{p_{1}}E_{1}
to a commutative diagram

(9.4.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{E}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0}^{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}^{\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}^{E}}

by defining
C^{\sharp}
to be the pushout of the diagram
\widetilde{E}_{0}\xleftarrow{\widetilde{p}_{0}}C^{\flat}\xrightarrow{\widetilde{p}_{1}}\widetilde{E}_{1}
, taken in the
\infty
-category of derived vector bundles over
C_{S}
, so that the inner diamond is (derived) Cartesian.

Dualizing (
9.4.2
), we get a commutative diagram

(9.4.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{E}}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{E}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{E}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{C^{\flat}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{E}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}^{\prime}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}^{\prime}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p^{\prime}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p^{\prime}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0}^{\widehat{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}^{\widehat{E}}}

where the inner diamond is Cartesian.

Given
\mathcal{K}_{i}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E_{i}}};\mathbf{Q})
for each
i\in\{0,1\}
, we define an isomorphism of vector spaces

(9.4.4)

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{C^{\flat}}:\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C^{\flat}}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C}^{\sharp}}}(\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{0}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{1}}(\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{p}_{0})+d(\widetilde{p}_{1})](d(\widetilde{p}_{0}))})

as the composite of the isomorphisms

\displaystyle\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C^{\flat}}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C^{\flat}}}((h_{0}^{E})^{*}\mathcal{K}_{0},(h_{1}^{E})^{!}\mathcal{K}_{1})

\displaystyle\text{(\lx@sectionsign\ref{ssec:main/fun}) }\implies

\displaystyle\xrightarrow{\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{C^{\flat}}}

\displaystyle\mathrm{CoCorr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C^{\flat}}}}(\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\widetilde{E}_{0}}((h_{0}^{E})^{*}\mathcal{K}_{0}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\widetilde{E}_{1}}((h_{1}^{E})^{!}\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{p}_{0})+d(\widetilde{p}_{1})](d(\widetilde{p}_{0}))})

\displaystyle\text{(\lx@sectionsign\ref{sssec: corr vs cocorr}) }\implies

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}}(\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\widetilde{E}_{0}}((h_{0}^{E})^{*}\mathcal{K}_{0}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\widetilde{E}_{1}}((h_{1}^{E})^{!}\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{p}_{0})+d(\widetilde{p}_{1})](d(\widetilde{p}_{0}))})

\displaystyle\text{(\lx@sectionsign\ref{ssec:main/funbase})}\implies

\displaystyle\cong

\displaystyle\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}}((h_{0}^{\widehat{E}})^{*}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{0}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}),(h_{1}^{\widehat{E}})^{!}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{1}}(\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{p}_{0})+d(\widetilde{p}_{1})](d(\widetilde{p}_{0}))})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}}(\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{0}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{1}}(\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{p}_{0})+d(\widetilde{p}_{1})](d(\widetilde{p}_{0}))}).

9.4.2.
Functoriality

We state and prove functorial properties of the Fourier transform of cohomological correspondences (
9.4.4
). Consider a diagram of maps of correspondences of derived Artin stacks

(9.4.5)

\textstyle{E_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{0}}
\textstyle{C^{\flat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\flat}}
\textstyle{E_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{1}}
\textstyle{F_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{D^{\flat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{q_{1}}
\textstyle{F_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{S_{0}}
\textstyle{C_{S}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{0}}
\scriptstyle{h_{1}}
\textstyle{S_{1}}

where
E_{i}
and
F_{i}
are derived vector bundles over
S_{i}
(for
i=0,1
), and
C^{\flat}
and
D^{\flat}
are derived vector bundles over
C_{S}
. All maps between derived vector bundles are assumed to be linear.

Let
\widetilde{E}_{i}\rightarrow C_{S}
,
\widetilde{F}_{i}\rightarrow C_{S}
and
\widetilde{f}_{i}:\widetilde{E}_{i}\rightarrow\widetilde{F}_{i}
be the base changes of
E_{i},F_{i}
and
f_{i}
along
h_{i}\colon C_{S}\rightarrow S_{i}
. Using the discussion in §
9.4.1
, we can canonically extend the upper part of the diagram (
9.4.5
) to a commutative diagram

(9.4.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{E}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{C}^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces D^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{F}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{F}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{D}^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{p}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0}^{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}^{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{f}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\diamond}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{q}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{q}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0}^{F}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{q}^{\prime}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{q}^{\prime}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}^{F}}

where the squares labeled by
\diamond
are derived Cartesian.

Since the leftmost parallelogram is derived Cartesian, the square
(C^{\flat},E_{0},D^{\flat},F_{0})
is pushable if and only if the square
(C^{\flat},\widetilde{E}_{0},D^{\flat},\widetilde{F}_{0})
is pushable. When any of these equivalent conditions holds, we have a pushforward map (for
\mathcal{K}_{i}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E_{i}}};\mathbf{Q})
),

(9.4.7)

f^{\flat}_{!}:\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C^{\flat}}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\to\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{D^{\flat}}}(f_{0!}\mathcal{K}_{0},f_{1!}\mathcal{K}_{1}).

The dual diagram to (
9.4.6
) is:

(9.4.8)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{D^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{F}}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{F}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{F}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{D}^{\flat}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{F}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{E}}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{E}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{E}_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{C}^{\flat}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{E}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{q}^{\prime}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{q}^{\prime}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}^{\sharp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{q^{\prime}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{q^{\prime}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0}^{\widehat{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{q}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{f}}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{q}}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{f}}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}^{\widehat{F}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{f}}^{\flat}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{f}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}^{\prime}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}^{\prime}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p^{\prime}_{1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{p^{\prime}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{0}^{\widehat{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{1}^{\widehat{E}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\widetilde{p}}_{1}}

Since the rightmost parallelogram is derived Cartesian, the square
(\widehat{D^{\sharp}},\widehat{F}_{1},\widehat{C^{\sharp}},\widehat{E}_{1})
is pullable if and only if the square
(\widehat{D^{\sharp}},\widehat{\widetilde{F}_{1}},\widehat{C^{\sharp}},\widehat{\widetilde{E}_{1}})
is pullable. When any of these equivalent conditions holds, we have a pullback map (for
\mathcal{K}_{i}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E_{i}}}};\mathbf{Q})
),

(9.4.9)

(\widehat{f^{\sharp}})^{*}:\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\to\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{D^{\sharp}}}}(\widehat{f}_{0}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{0},\widehat{f}_{1}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{1}{\scriptstyle\langle-\delta_{\widehat{f^{\sharp}}}\rangle}).

Moreover, by
[
FYZ22
, Lemma 7.2.1]
,
f^{\flat}
is left pushable if and only if
\widehat{f^{\sharp}}
is right pullable.

Proposition 9.4.1
.

Assume the diagram (
9.4.8
) is globally presented.

(1) Suppose the map of correspondences
f^{\flat}:C^{\flat}\to D^{\flat}
is left pushable. Let
\mathcal{K}_{i}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E_{i}}};\mathbf{Q})
for
i=0,1
. Then the following diagram commutes:

(9.4.10)

\textstyle{\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C^{\flat}}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{C^{\flat}}}
\scriptstyle{f^{\flat}_{!}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}}(\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{0}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{1}}(\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{p}_{0})+d(\widetilde{p}_{1})](d(\widetilde{p}_{0}))})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\widehat{f}^{\sharp})^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{D^{\flat}}}(f_{0!}\mathcal{K}_{0},f_{1!}\mathcal{K}_{1})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathbb{T}_{[d(f_{0})]}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{D^{\flat}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{D^{\sharp}}}}(\widehat{f}_{0}^{*}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{0}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}),\widehat{f}_{1}^{*}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{E_{1}}(\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{q}_{0})+d(\widetilde{q}_{1})+d(f_{0})-d(f_{1})](d(\widetilde{q}_{0}))})}

Here we use
[
FYZ22
, Lemma 7.2.2]
to match the differences of the twists that appear in the right vertical map.

(2) Suppose the map of correspondences
f^{\flat}:C^{\flat}\to D^{\flat}
is right pullable. Let
\mathcal{K}_{i}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{F_{i}}};\mathbf{Q})
for
i=0,1
. Then the following diagram commutes

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{D^{\flat}}}(\mathcal{K}_{0},\mathcal{K}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{D^{\sharp}}}}(\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{F_{0}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{F_{1}}(\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{q}_{0})+d(\widetilde{q}_{1})](d(\widetilde{q}_{0}))})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C^{\flat}}}(f_{0}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{0},f_{1}^{*}\mathcal{K}_{1})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{C^{\sharp}}}}(\widehat{f}_{0!}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{F_{0}}(\mathcal{K}_{0}),\widehat{f}_{1!}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{F_{1}}(\mathcal{K}_{1}){\scriptstyle[d(\widetilde{q}_{0})+d(\widetilde{q}_{1})](d(\widetilde{q}_{0}))})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{D^{\flat}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(f^{\flat})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widehat{f}^{\sharp})_{!}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathbb{T}_{[d(f_{0})](d(f_{0}))}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{C^{\flat}}}

Proof.

The proof of
[
FYZ22
, Proposition 7.2.4]
works verbatim.
∎

9.5.
Homogeneous arithmetic Fourier transform

In this section we lift (a homogeneous variant of) the
arithmetic Fourier transform
of
[
FYZ22
, §8]
from
\ell
-adic Borel–Moore homology to Chow groups.

9.5.1.
Étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundles

Let
T
be a derived Artin stack locally of finite type over a field.
Let
V\rightarrow T
be an étale locally free
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle of rank
d
(thus, the datum of
V
is equivalent to that of an étale
\GL_{d}(\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times})
-torsor).

Define
{}^{\dagger}{V}
to be the stack quotient
[V/\underline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}^{\times}]
, where
\underline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}^{\times}
is the discrete group scheme over
T
with value
\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}
.
Let
\widehat{V}\rightarrow T
be the dual
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space, i.e., at the level of étale sheaves over
T
we have

\widehat{V}\coloneqq\mathcal{H}om_{\mathbf{F}_{q}}(V,\underline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}).

Note that
\widehat{\widehat{V}}\cong V
. We have an “evaluation” map

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev\colon{}^{\dagger}{V}\times_{T}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{V}}\rightarrow[\mathbf{F}_{q}/\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}].}

9.5.2.
Homogeneous arithmetic Fourier transform

Let
\xi
be the function on
\mathbf{F}_{q}
defined as

\xi(x)=\begin{cases}q-1&x=0,\\
-1&x\neq 0.\end{cases}

As
\xi
is invariant under the scaling action of
\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}
,
\xi
descends to a function on the quotient stack
[\mathbf{F}_{q}/\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}]
. Its significance is the following: if
\psi
is any nontrivial additive character of
\underline{\mathbf{F}}_{q}
, then the function on
[\mathbf{F}_{q}/\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}]
obtained by averaging
\psi
over
\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}
is
\xi
.

Now consider the diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{V}\times_{T}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[\mathbf{F}_{q}/\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{V}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{V}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces T}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pr_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\ev}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widehat{\pi}}

We define the
homogeneous arithmetic Fourier transform
to be the map

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}_{V}\colon\CH_{*}({}^{\dagger}{V})\rightarrow\CH_{*}({}^{\dagger}{\widehat{V}})

given by

\alpha\mapsto(-1)^{d}\pr_{1*}(\pr_{0}^{!}(\alpha)\cap\ev^{*}\xi),

where
d
is the rank of
V
as an
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space over
T
.
Here, we regard the locally constant function
\xi
as an element of
\CH^{0}([\mathbf{F}_{q}/\mathbf{F}_{q}^{\times}])
.
We also used the fact that the projections
\pr_{i}
are finite étale so that the pushforward and pullback maps are defined.

Similarly, we consider a variant in Chow cohomology (§
3.5
):

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}\colon\CH^{*}({}^{\dagger}{V};\mathbf{Q})\rightarrow\CH^{*}({}^{\dagger}{\widehat{V}};\mathbf{Q})

given by

\alpha\mapsto(-1)^{d}\pr_{1!}(\pr_{0}^{*}(\alpha)\cup\ev^{*}\xi).

9.5.3.
Basic properties

The following properties of the homogeneous arithmetic Fourier transform follow by the same arguments as in the non-homogeneous case (which are found in
[
FYZ22
, §8.2]
).

Notation 9.5.1
.

For
\alpha\in\CH_{i}({}^{\dagger}{V})
and
\beta\in\CH^{j}({}^{\dagger}{V})
, we write

\langle\alpha,\beta\rangle\coloneqq\pi_{*}(\alpha\cap\beta)\in\CH_{i-j}(T)

where
\pi\colon{}^{\dagger}{V}\rightarrow T
is the projection.

Lemma 9.5.2

(Plancherel property)
.

Let
\alpha_{1}\in\CH_{i}({}^{\dagger}{V})
and
\beta_{2}\in\CH^{j}({}^{\dagger}{\widehat{V}})
. Then

\langle\alpha_{1},\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}(\beta_{2})\rangle=\langle\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}(\alpha_{1}),\beta_{2}\rangle\in\CH_{i-j}(T).

Lemma 9.5.3

(Involutivity)
.

We have
\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}_{\widehat{V}}\circ\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}_{V}=q^{d}
where
d
is the rank of
V
.

9.6.
Compatibility of motivic and arithmetic Fourier transforms

We establish the compatibility of the motivic Fourier transform with arithmetic Fourier transform under the motivic sheaf-cycle correspondence.

9.6.1.
Setup

Let
Y
be a derived Artin stack locally of finite type over a field.
Let
p\colon E\to Y
be a vector bundle
22
22

22

i.e., a derived vector bundle of amplitude
[0,0]
.
. Suppose
c=(c_{0},c_{1})\colon C\to Y\times Y
is a correspondence of derived Artin stacks and we are given an isomorphism of vector bundles over
C

(9.6.1)

\iota\colon c_{0}^{*}E\cong c_{1}^{*}E.

Let
C_{E}
be the total space of
c_{0}^{*}E\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\cong}}c_{1}^{*}E
. For
i\in\{0,1\}
we let
e_{i}\colon C_{E}\cong c_{i}^{*}E\to E
be the corresponding projection map. Then we have a map of correspondences

(9.6.2)

\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pr}
\textstyle{C_{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pr_{C}}
\scriptstyle{e_{0}}
\scriptstyle{e_{1}}
\textstyle{E\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\pr}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{0}}
\scriptstyle{c_{1}}
\textstyle{Y}

such that both squares are Cartesian.

The above data induces a correspondence
\widehat{e}\colon C_{\widehat{E}}\to\widehat{E}\times\widehat{E}
by passing to the dual vector bundles.

Consider the Frobenius twisted correspondence map
c^{(1)}=(\Frob\circ c_{0},c_{1})\colon C^{(1)}\rightarrow Y\times Y
. Similarly, we define
C^{(1)}_{E}
(a self-correspondence of
E
) and
C^{(1)}_{\widehat{E}}
(a self-correspondence of
\widehat{E}
). Recall notation (§
6.4.2
)

(9.6.3)

\Sht(C)\coloneqq\Fix(C^{(1)}),\quad\Sht(C_{E})\coloneqq\Fix(C^{(1)}_{E}),\quad\Sht(C_{\widehat{E}})\coloneqq\Fix(C^{(1)}_{\widehat{E}}).

The projections

(9.6.4)

\pi:\Sht(C_{E})\to\Sht(C),\quad\widehat{\pi}:\Sht(C_{\widehat{E}})\to\Sht(C)

are étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundles over
\Sht(C)
that are dual to each other.

9.6.2.

Let
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot},\mathrm{gm}}({{}^{\dagger}{E}};\mathbf{Q})
and
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C_{E}}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}{{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle}})
.

On one hand, we can form the Frobenius-twisted trace (§
6.4.3
)

(9.6.5)

\Tr^{\Sht}(\mathfrak{c})\coloneqq\Tr(\mathfrak{c}^{(1)})\in\CH_{i}({}^{\dagger}{\Sht(C_{E})})

where
\mathfrak{c}^{(1)}
is the cohomological correspondence

(9.6.6)

\mathfrak{c}^{(1)}\colon e_{0}^{*}\Frob_{E}^{*}\mathcal{K}\cong e_{0}^{*}\mathcal{K}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{c}}e_{1}^{!}\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle}

supported on
{}^{\dagger}{C_{E}^{(1)}}
(see §
6.4.2
).

On the other hand, we can first apply the homogeneous motivic Fourier transform to get a cohomological correspondence

\FT_{C_{E}}(\mathfrak{c})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C_{\widehat{E}}}}(\FT_{E}(\mathcal{K}),\FT_{E}(\mathcal{K}))

given by the composite

(9.6.7)

\widehat{e}_{0}^{*}\FT_{E}(\mathcal{K})\cong\FT_{C_{E}}(e_{0}^{*}\mathcal{K})\xrightarrow{\FT_{C_{E}}(\mathfrak{c})}\FT_{C_{E}}(e_{1}^{!}\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle})\cong\widehat{e}_{1}^{!}\FT_{E}(\mathcal{K}){{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle}},

where we used the commutativity of the Fourier transform with base change (§
8.2.6
).
We can then (using that
\FT(\mathcal{K})
is geometric, thanks to Corollary
8.4.2
) form the Frobenius-twisted trace

(9.6.8)

\Tr^{\Sht}(\FT_{C_{E}}(\mathfrak{c}))\coloneqq\Tr(\FT_{C_{E}}(\mathfrak{c})^{(1)})\in\CH_{i}({}^{\dagger}{\Sht(C_{\widehat{E}})}).

The two constructions (
9.6.5
) and (
9.6.8
) agree up to the arithmetic Fourier transform:

Theorem 9.6.1
.

In the above situation, we have

(9.6.9)

\Tr^{\Sht}(\FT_{C_{E}}(\mathfrak{c}))=\FT^{\mathrm{arith}}_{\Sht(C_{E})}(\Tr^{\Sht}(\mathfrak{c}))\in\CH_{i}({}^{\dagger}{\Sht(C_{\widehat{E}})}).

Proof.

The version for
\ell
-adic coefficients is
[
FYZ22
, Theorem 8.3.2]
, and the proof here is similar with a few modifications. The renormalized homogeneous Fourier transform (resp. arithmetic homogeneous Fourier transform) is the composition of three steps:

(a)

pullback along
{}^{\dagger}{E}\leftarrow{}^{\dagger}{E}\times_{Y}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}
(resp. pullback along
{}^{\dagger}{\Sht(E)}\leftarrow{}^{\dagger}{\Sht(E)}\times_{\Sht(C)}{}^{\dagger}{\Sht(\widehat{E})}
),

(b)

tensor with
\mathscr{P}_{E}[r-1]
(resp. multiply by
(-1)^{r}\ev^{*}\xi
) where
r=\rk(E)
,

(c)

pushforward along
{}^{\dagger}{E}\times_{Y}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\rightarrow{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}
(resp. pushforward along
{}^{\dagger}{\Sht(E)}\times_{\Sht(C)}{}^{\dagger}{\Sht(\widehat{E})}\rightarrow{}^{\dagger}{\Sht(\widehat{E})}
).

It suffices to show that each of these steps is compatible with the formation of the trace. The first two are easy:

•

Since the pullback in (a) is smooth, the compatibility there follows from Proposition
6.2.2
.

•

The formation of trace takes the operation of tensoring with a self-correspondence of a local system to the operation of multiplication by the trace. Hence (b) follows from the computation that the Frobenius trace function of
\mathscr{P}_{E}
is
-\ev^{*}\xi
.

For (c), we need to show that pushforward through the projection
\pr_{1}\colon{}^{\dagger}{E}\times_{Y}{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}\rightarrow{}^{\dagger}{\widehat{E}}
commutes with formation of trace. From the map of correspondences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{C_{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{e}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}^{\dagger}{e}_{1}}

we have maps of correspondences

\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C_{E}}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle})\xrightarrow{f^{*}}\mathrm{Corr}_{C_{E}}(f^{*}\mathcal{K},f^{*}\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle})

and

\mathrm{Corr}_{C_{E}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle})\xrightarrow{f_{!}}\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{C_{E}}}(f_{!}\mathcal{K},f_{!}\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle}).

The endofunctor
f_{!}f^{*}\colon\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E}};\mathbf{Q})\rightarrow\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot}}({{}^{\dagger}{E}};\mathbf{Q})
is given by tensoring with
f_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{E}
. Since
f
is a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-torsor, this implies that
\Tr(f_{!}f^{*}\cc)=(q-1)\Tr(\cc)
, which agrees with
\Fix(f)_{*}\Fix(f)^{!}(\Tr(\cc))
since
\Fix(f)
is a
\mathbf{G}_{m}(\mathbf{F}_{q})
-torsor. Replacing
\cc
by
f^{*}\cc
, it therefore suffices to show that the projection
\pr_{1}\colon E\times_{Y}\widehat{E}\rightarrow\widehat{E}
commutes with formation of trace. This follows from Lemma
9.6.2
.
∎

Lemma 9.6.2
.

In the situation of (
9.6.2
), let
\mathcal{K}\in\mathcal{D}_{\mathrm{mot},\mathrm{gm}}({E};\mathbf{Q})
and
\mathfrak{c}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{C_{E}}(\mathcal{K},\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle})
. Let
\Sht(\pr)\colon\Sht(C_{E})\to\Sht(C)
be the induced map on fixed points of
C_{E}^{(1)}
and
C^{(1)}
, which is an étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle (in particular a finite morphism). Then

(9.6.10)

\Tr(\pr_{C!}(\mathfrak{c}))=\Sht(\pr)_{*}(\Tr(\mathfrak{c}^{(1)}))\in\CH_{i}(\Sht(C)).

Proof.

Note that this does not follow from Proposition
6.2.1
since
\pr
is far from proper. The analogous result for
\ell
-adic sheaves is
[
FYZ22
, Lemma 8.3.3]
, and the proof here is essentially the same: compactify the map of correspondences, use the compatibility of trace and pushforward on the compactification, and then show that the boundary contribution vanishes. As some references need to be replaced, we will spell out the argument.

We compactify the map of correspondences (
9.6.2
) to

(9.6.11)

\textstyle{\overline{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\pr}}
\textstyle{C_{\overline{E}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\pr}_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{e}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\overline{e}_{1}}
\textstyle{\overline{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\overline{\pr}}
\textstyle{Y}
\textstyle{C\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{c_{0}}
\scriptstyle{c_{1}}
\textstyle{Y}

where
\overline{E}\coloneqq\mathbf{P}(E\oplus\mathcal{O})\to Y
and
C_{\overline{E}}\coloneqq\mathbf{P}(c_{0}^{*}E\oplus\mathcal{O})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\iota}}{{\cong}}\mathbf{P}(c_{1}^{*}E\oplus\mathcal{O})\to C
is the pullback projective bundle over
C
. Then
C_{\overline{E}}
is a self-correspondence of
\overline{E}
with a proper map to
C
. Let
E_{\infty}\coloneqq\overline{E}-E
be the divisor at infinity, which is isomorphic to
\mathbf{P}(E)
. Similarly define
C_{E_{\infty}}\coloneqq C_{\overline{E}}-C_{E}
, which is a self-correspondence of
E_{\infty}
. Let
C^{(1)}_{\overline{E}}
and
C^{(1)}_{E_{\infty}}
be the twists by Frobenius as in §
6.4.2
. Since the vertical maps in (
9.6.11
) are proper, Proposition
6.2.1
implies that
\overline{\pr}_{C!}
is compatible with formation of traces.

Let
j:E\hookrightarrow\overline{E}
and
j_{C}:C_{E}\hookrightarrow C_{\overline{E}}
be the open inclusions. The map of correspondences

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces E}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{E}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces C_{\overline{E}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{E}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j_{C}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces j}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{e}_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{e}_{1}}

has both squares Cartesian, so it is left pushable. Therefore the pushforward cohomological correspondence

(9.6.12)

\overline{\mathfrak{c}}\coloneqq j_{C!}(\mathfrak{c})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{C_{\overline{E}}}(j_{!}\mathcal{K},j_{!}\mathcal{K}{\scriptstyle\langle-i\rangle})

is defined. It remains to show that
\Sht(\pr)_{*}\Tr(\overline{c})=\Sht(\pr)_{*}\Tr(\mathfrak{c})
, which amounts to the vanishing of the contribution from the boundary correspondence. The rest of the argument is exactly the same as the corresponding step in the proof of
[
FYZ22
, Lemma 8.3.3]
, except using Theorem
7.5.1
instead of
[
Var07
]
to see that the contribution from the boundary correspondence vanishes, because
C^{(1)}_{\overline{E}}
is contracting near
E_{\infty}
by Example
7.2.2
.
∎

10.
Generic modularity of higher theta series

In this section we will assemble the preceding theory to prove the main result, Theorem
1.2.1
.

10.1.
Notation

We fix the following notation throughout the section.

We let
\nu\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X
be an étale double cover of smooth projective curves over a finite field
\mathbf{F}_{q}
of characteristic
p>2
, and
\sigma\colon X^{\prime}\rightarrow X^{\prime}
be the non-trivial automorphism over
X
. We let
\Frob
denote the
q
-power Frobenius.

For a torsion coherent sheaf
Q
on a curve
X^{\prime}
we let
D_{Q}
be its scheme-theoretic support, viewed as a divisor on
X^{\prime}
, and
|Q|\subset X^{\prime}
its set-theoretic support.

Let
n\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 1}
. The (smooth, classical,
1
-Artin) stack
\Bun_{GU(n)}
parametrizes triples
(\mathcal{F},\mathfrak{L},h)
, where
\mathcal{F}
is a vector bundle on
X^{\prime}
of rank
n
,
\mathfrak{L}
is a line bundle on
X
, and
h:\mathcal{F}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{F}^{\vee}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L}
is an
\mathfrak{L}
-twisted Hermitian structure (i.e.,
\sigma^{*}h^{\vee}=h
). The corresponding moduli space of shtukas is denoted
\Sht_{GU(n)}
.

10.2.
Higher theta series

We briefly summarize the construction of higher theta series on
\Sht_{GU(n)}
, which can be found in
[
FYZ21b
, §4]
. Let
m\in\mathbf{Z}_{\geq 1}
. The stack
\Bun_{GU^{-}(2m)}
parametrizes triples
(\mathcal{G},\mathfrak{M},h)
, where
\mathcal{G}
is a vector bundle on
X^{\prime}
of rank
2m
,
\mathfrak{M}
is a line bundle on
X
, and
h:\mathcal{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\vee}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{M}
is an
\mathfrak{M}
-twisted
skew-Hermitian
structure (i.e.,
\sigma^{*}h^{\vee}=-h
). Alternatively, we can think of
h
as an
\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}}
-bilinear perfect pairing

(10.2.1)

(\cdot,\cdot)_{h}:\mathcal{G}\times\sigma^{*}\mathcal{G}\to\nu^{*}(\mathfrak{M}\otimes\omega_{X})

satisfying
(\sigma^{*}\beta,\sigma^{*}\alpha)_{h}=-\sigma^{*}(\alpha,\beta)_{h}
for local sections
\alpha
and
\beta
of
\mathcal{G}
respectively.

Let
\Bun_{\widetilde{P}_{m}}
be the moduli stack of quadruples
(\mathcal{G},\mathfrak{M},h,\mathcal{E})
where
(\mathcal{G},\mathfrak{M},h)\in\Bun_{GU^{-}(2m)}
, and
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{G}
is a Lagrangian sub-bundle (i.e.,
\mathcal{E}
has rank
m
and the composition
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{G}\xrightarrow{h}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{G}^{\vee}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{M}\to\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{M}
is zero). Thus
\widetilde{P}_{m}
corresponds to the Siegel parabolic of
GU^{-}(2m)
.

Assume now that
1\leq m\leq n
. In
[
FYZ21b
, §4.6]
, we defined for each
r\geq 0
and
m\leq n
a
higher theta series
, which is a function

\widetilde{Z}^{r}_{m}\colon\Bun_{\widetilde{P}_{m}}(k)\to\CH_{r(n-m)}(\Sht^{r}_{GU(n)}).

We repeat the brief recap of the definition of
\widetilde{Z}^{r}_{m}
from
[
FYZ22
, §2]
.
Let
(\mathcal{G},\mathfrak{M},h,\mathcal{E})\in\Bun_{\widetilde{P}_{m}}(k)
and set
\mathfrak{L}\coloneqq\omega_{X}\otimes\mathfrak{M}
. Let
\Sht^{r}_{U(n),\mathfrak{L}}\subset\Sht^{r}_{GU(n)}
be the moduli stack of rank
n
Hermitian shtukas

\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}=((x_{i}),(\mathcal{F}_{i}),(f_{i}),\varphi:\mathcal{F}_{r}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}{}^{\tau}\mathcal{F}_{0})

on
X^{\prime}
with
r
legs and similitude line bundle
\mathfrak{L}
. For a vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
on
X^{\prime}
of rank
m
, the special cycle
\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}
parametrizes a point
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
of
\Sht^{r}_{U(n),\mathfrak{L}}
, and maps
t_{i}:\mathcal{E}\to\mathcal{F}_{i}
for each
0\leqslant i\leqslant r
, compatible with the shtuka structure on
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
. For a Hermitian map
a:\mathcal{E}\to\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L}
, let
\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(a)
be the open-closed substack of
\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}
consisting of
(\mathcal{F}_{\bullet},t_{\bullet})
such that the Hermitian form on
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}
induces the Hermitian map
a
on
\mathcal{E}
via
t_{\bullet}
. Let
\zeta:\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(a)\to\Sht^{r}_{U(n),\mathfrak{L}}\subset\Sht^{r}_{GU(n)}
be the map forgetting
t_{\bullet}
, which is known to be finite
[
FYZ21a
, Proposition 7.5]
and unramified.

In
[
FYZ21b
, Definition 4.8]
there is constructed a
virtual fundamental class

[\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(a)]\in\CH_{r(n-m)}(\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(a))
, although the interpretation as a derived fundamental class in
[
FYZ21b
, §5,6]
will be more useful in the proofs. Pushing forward along
\zeta
, we get Chow classes

\zeta_{*}[\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(a)]\in\CH_{r(n-m)}(\Sht^{r}_{U(n),\mathfrak{L}}).

The value of
\widetilde{Z}^{r}_{m}
on
(\mathcal{G},\mathfrak{M},h,\mathcal{E})
(recall
\mathfrak{M}=\omega_{X}^{-1}\otimes\mathfrak{L}
), which we henceforth abbreviate as
(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E})
, is defined as

(10.2.2)

\widetilde{Z}^{r}_{m}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E})=\chi(\det\mathcal{E})q^{n(\deg\mathcal{E}-\deg\mathfrak{L}-\deg\omega_{X})/2}\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(k)}\psi(\langle{e_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E}},a}\rangle)\zeta_{*}[\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(a)],

where:

•

\chi:\Pic_{X^{\prime}}(k)\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}
is a character whose restriction to
\Pic_{X}(k)
is the
n
th power of the quadratic character
\Pic_{X}(k)\to\{\pm 1\}
corresponding to the double cover
X^{\prime}/X
by class field theory.

•

\psi:\mathbf{F}_{q}\to\overline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}
is a nontrivial additive character.

•

The sum is indexed over
\mathcal{A}_{\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{L}}(k)
, the set of Hermitian maps
a:\mathcal{E}\to\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L}
.

•

Let
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}=\mathcal{G}/\mathcal{E}
. The pairing
(\cdot,\cdot)_{h}
in (
10.2.1
) induces a perfect pairing
\mathcal{E}\times\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\prime}\to\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L}
, which identifies
\mathcal{E}^{\prime}
with
\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{*}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L}
. We thus have a short exact sequence

\textstyle{0\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{E}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathcal{G}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{*}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{0}

and
e_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E}}\in\Ext^{1}(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{*}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L},\mathcal{E})
is its extension class.

•

The pairing
\langle{-,-}\rangle
is the Serre duality pairing between
\Ext^{1}(\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{*}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L},\mathcal{E})
and
\Hom(\mathcal{E},\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\vee}\otimes\nu^{*}\mathfrak{L})
.

10.3.
The generic modularity theorem

The Modularity Conjecture of
[
FYZ21b
]
predicts that
\widetilde{Z}_{m}^{r}
descends to a function on
\Bun_{GU^{-}(2m)}(k)
. As explained just after
[
FYZ21b
, Conjecture 4.15]
, this can be reformulated as the assertion that
\widetilde{Z}^{r}_{m}(\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E})
is actually independent of the choice of Lagrangian sub-bundle
\mathcal{E}\subset\mathcal{G}
. We will prove this statement after restriction to the generic locus (cf. §
1.2.2
):

Theorem 10.3.1
.

For any
\mathcal{G}\in\Bun_{GU^{-}(2m)}(k)
and any Lagrangian sub-bundles
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}\subset\mathcal{G}
, we have

(10.3.1)

\widetilde{Z}_{m}^{r}(\mathcal{G},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1})=\widetilde{Z}_{m}^{r}(\mathcal{G},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2})\in\CH_{r(n-m)}(\Sht^{r}_{GU(n)}\times_{(X^{\prime})^{r}}\eta^{r}).

where
\eta^{r}
is as in §
1.2.2
.

10.3.1.
Reduction to transverse Lagrangians

As explained in
[
FYZ22
, §2.2]
, Theorem
10.3.1
is reduced to the case where the sub-bundles
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1},\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}
are
transverse
in the sense that their intersection within
\mathcal{G}
is the zero section. Henceforth we assume this to be the case.

10.3.2.
Reduction to Harder–Narasimhan truncations

Given a Harder–Narasimhan polygon
\mu
for
GU(n)
, we have a Harder–Narasimhan truncation
\Sht^{r,\leq\mu}_{GU(n)}\hookrightarrow\Sht^{r}_{GU(n)}
. Because

\CH_{*}(\Sht^{r}_{GU(n)}\times_{(X^{\prime})^{r}}\eta^{r})\cong\varprojlim_{\mu}\CH_{*}(\Sht^{r,\leq\mu}_{GU(n)}\times_{(X^{\prime})^{r}}\eta^{r}),

it suffices to show (
10.3.1
) after restriction to this truncation. Henceforth we fix a Harder–Narasimhan polygon
\mu
and write
S=\Bun_{GU(n)}^{\leqslant\mu}
for the corresponding open substack of
\Bun_{GU(n)}
. As in
[
FYZ22
, §9.1.2]
, we write
\Hk_{S}^{r}\coloneqq h_{0}^{-1}(S)\cap\ldots\cap h_{r}^{-1}(S)
where
h_{i}\colon\Hk_{GU(n)}^{r}\rightarrow\Bun_{GU(n)}
are the “leg maps”.

For a space over
\Bun_{GU(n)}
such as
\Sht_{GU(n)}^{r}
or the special cycles on it, we write
(-)^{\leq\mu}
for the pullback to
S
.

10.4.
Transverse Lagrangians ansatz

Let
\mathcal{G}\in\Bun_{GU^{-}(2m)}(k)
. For notational simplicity, we assume the similitude line bundle of
\mathcal{G}
is trivial, therefore the skew-Hermitian form reads
h_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{G}^{*}
. The general case has the same content.

Thanks to the transversality assumption from §
10.3.1
, we have a commutative diagram (cf.
[
FYZ22
, §2.3.3]
)

(10.4.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}^{\sharp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{12}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces b_{21}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces i_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota_{1}}

where the horizontal rows and diagonal sequences are short exact sequences of coherent sheaves on
X^{\prime}
. Here
b_{12}
is the composition

\widetilde{\mathcal{E}_{1}}\rightarrow\mathcal{G}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\sim}}{{\to}}\sigma^{*}\mathcal{G}^{*}\rightarrow\sigma^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{*}.

The map
b_{21}
is defined similarly. The maps
\iota_{1}
and
\iota_{2}
are isomorphisms of torsion sheaves on
X^{\prime}
. As in
[
FYZ22
, §2.3.3]
, the duality between
Q_{1}
and
Q_{2}
equips
Q
with two Hermitian structures
h_{12}\colon Q\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{*}Q^{*}
and
h_{21}\colon Q\xrightarrow{\sim}\sigma^{*}Q^{*}
, related by
h_{12}=-h_{21}
.

For each
i\in\{1,2\}
let
\mathcal{E}_{i}\hookrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{E}_{i}}
be a sub-sheaf with cokernel a torsion coherent sheaf
\mathcal{T}_{i}
on
X^{\prime}
. Let
\mathcal{T}^{*}_{i}=\RHom(\mathcal{T}_{i},\mathcal{O}_{X^{\prime}})[1]
be its dual torsion sheaf on
X^{\prime}
. Therefore,
\widetilde{\mathcal{E}_{i}}
is the saturation of
\mathcal{E}_{i}
in
\mathcal{G}
, and we have the diagram below

(10.4.2)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{E}_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\widetilde{\mathcal{E}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{G}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{*}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}_{2}^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathcal{T}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\sigma^{*}\mathcal{T}_{2}^{*}}

where the arrows are labeled by their cokernels.

10.4.1.
Assumptions on
\mathcal{T}_{1}
and
\mathcal{T}_{2}

We make the following assumptions:

(a)

The supports
|Q|,|\mathcal{T}_{1}|,|\mathcal{T}_{2}|
are disjoint after mapping to
X
.

(b)

For all
\mathcal{F}\in S(\overline{k})=\Bun_{U(n)}^{\leq\mu}(\overline{k})
we have for
i=1,2

(10.4.3)

\Ext^{1}_{X_{\overline{k}}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\mathcal{E}_{i}^{*})=0.

These conditions can always be arranged, as discussed in
[
FYZ22
, Remark 10.1.1]
. Note that by the dualities in
[
FYZ22
, (10.1.3)]
, (
10.4.3
) is equivalent to

(10.4.4)

\Hom_{X_{\overline{k}}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}_{i})=0

for all
\mathcal{F}\in\Bun_{U(n)}^{\leq\mu}(\overline{k})
and
i=1,2
.

Let

(10.4.5)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{1}\coloneqq Q^{*}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{1}^{*}\oplus\sigma^{*}\mathcal{T}_{2},

(10.4.6)

\displaystyle\widetilde{Q}_{2}\coloneqq\sigma^{*}Q\oplus\sigma^{*}\mathcal{T}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{2}^{*}.

From (
10.4.2
) and the disjointness assumption in §
10.4.1
, we have short exact sequences

(10.4.7)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}_{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{1}^{*}\rightarrow\widetilde{Q}_{1}\rightarrow 0,

(10.4.8)

\displaystyle 0\rightarrow\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}_{1}\rightarrow\mathcal{E}_{2}^{*}\rightarrow\widetilde{Q}_{2}\rightarrow 0.

10.5.
Moduli spaces

We will use the same moduli spaces as in
[
FYZ22
, §9.1, §9.2]
:

(a)

For
i\in\{0,r\}
:
U_{i},V_{i},W_{i}
, which are derived vector bundles over
S
; and their respective dual derived vector bundles
W_{i}^{\perp},\widehat{V}_{i},U_{i}^{\perp}
.

(b)

For
i\in\{0,r\}
:
\widetilde{U}_{i},\widetilde{V}_{i},\widetilde{W}_{i}
, which are derived vector bundles over
\Hk_{S}^{r}
obtained by pulling back
U_{i},V_{i},W_{i}
respectively along
h_{i}
; and their respective dual derived vector bundles
\widetilde{W}_{i}^{\perp},\widehat{\widetilde{V}}_{i},\widetilde{U}_{i}^{\perp}
.

(c)

The Hecke stacks
\Hk_{U}^{\perp},\Hk_{U}^{\sharp},\Hk_{V}^{\flat},\Hk_{V}^{\sharp},\Hk_{W}^{\flat},\Hk_{W}^{\sharp}
, which are derived vector bundles over
\Hk_{S}^{r}
; and their respective dual derived vector bundles
\Hk_{W^{\perp}}^{\sharp},\Hk_{W^{\perp}}^{\flat},\Hk_{\widehat{V}}^{\sharp},\Hk_{\widehat{V}}^{\flat},\Hk_{U^{\perp}}^{\sharp},\Hk_{U^{\perp}}^{\flat}
.

We give an informal summary of the definitions. Recall that for an animated
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-algebra
R
, an
R
-point of
S
is a Hermitian bundle
\mathcal{F}
on
X^{\prime}_{R}
. In these terms,

•

The fiber of
U_{i}
over
\mathcal{F}\in S(R)
is
\RHom_{X_{R}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\mathcal{E}_{1}^{*}\otimes R)
.

•

The fiber of
V_{i}
over
\mathcal{F}\in S(R)
is
\RHom_{X_{R}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\widetilde{Q}_{1}\otimes R)
.

•

The fiber of
W_{i}
over
\mathcal{F}\in S(R)
is
\RHom_{X_{R}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}_{2}[1]\otimes R)
.

•

The fiber of
U_{i}^{\perp}
over
\mathcal{F}\in S(R)
is
\RHom_{X_{R}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\mathcal{E}_{2}^{*}\otimes R)
.

•

The fiber of
\widehat{V}_{i}
over
\mathcal{F}\in S(R)
is
\RHom_{X_{R}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\widetilde{Q}_{2}\otimes R)
.

•

The fiber of
W_{i}^{\perp}
over
\mathcal{F}\in S(R)
is
\RHom_{X_{R}^{\prime}}(\mathcal{F}^{*},\sigma^{*}\mathcal{E}_{1}[1]\otimes R)
.

Note that the definitions of the six spaces above are, in fact, independent of
i
. However, it the notation is useful for indexing purposes.

For an animated
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-algebra
R
, an
R
-point of
\Hk_{S}^{r}
is a sequence of modifications of Hermitian bundles
(\mathcal{F}_{0}\dashrightarrow\ldots\dashrightarrow\mathcal{F}_{r})
on
X_{R}^{\prime}
that we abbreviate
\mathcal{F}_{\star}
. For
i\in\{0,r\}
and
?\in\{U_{i},V_{i},W_{i},U_{i}^{\perp},\widehat{V}_{i},W_{i}^{\perp}\}
, the fiber of
\widetilde{?}
over
(\mathcal{F}_{\star})\in\Hk_{S}^{r}(R)
is obtained by replacing
\mathcal{F}
with
\mathcal{F}_{i}
in the descriptions of
?(R)
above.

From
\mathcal{F}_{\star}\in\Hk_{S}^{r}(R)
we can define perfect complexes
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\flat}
and
F_{\bullet}^{\sharp}
on
X_{R}^{\prime}
as in
[
FYZ22
, §9.1.3, §9.2.2]
. For
?\in\{U,V,W\}
the fiber of
\Hk_{?}^{\flat}
is obtained by replacing
\mathcal{F}
with
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\flat}
in the description of
?(R)
above, while the fiber of
\Hk_{?}^{\sharp}
over
\mathcal{F}_{\star}\in\Hk_{S}^{r}(R)
is obtained by replacing
\mathcal{F}
with
\mathcal{F}_{\bullet}^{\sharp}
in the description of
?(R)
above.

Remark 10.5.1
.

The vanishing assumption (
10.4.3
) implies that
U,V
and
W
are all classical vector bundles over
S
, and we have a short exact sequence of classical vector bundles over
S

(10.5.1)

0\to U_{i}\to V_{i}\to W_{i}\to 0.

Similarly, we have a short exact sequence of classical vector bundles over
S

(10.5.2)

0\to U^{\bot}_{i}\to\widehat{V}_{i}\to W^{\bot}_{i}\to 0.

Thus we have a pair of commutative diagrams:

(10.5.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{U}^{\flat}}

\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g_{0}^{\perp}}
