\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widetilde{c}_{0}^{\prime})^{\perp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h_{r}^{W^{\bot}}}

In each diagram:

•

The maps in the columns come from exact triangles of perfect complexes.

•

The three diamonds in the middle are derived Cartesian.

•

The four parallelograms on the left and right sides are derived Cartesian.

The diagram on the right is dual to the diagram on the left. The duality exchanges
U
with
W^{\perp}
,
V
with
\widehat{V}
, and
W
with
U^{\perp}
, and exchanges
\flat
and
\sharp
superscripts. Sample examples of dual morphisms are colored with the same color. By
[
FYZ22
, §9.3]
, each of these diagrams is globally presented, so that we may apply the results of §
9.2
to them.

10.6.
Calculation of motivic Fourier transforms

We refer to the diagrams in (
10.5.3
). By
[
FYZ22
, Corollary 9.1.4]
, the map
a_{r}
is quasi-smooth, hence it has a relative fundamental class, which defines as in §
6.3
a cohomological correspondence

(10.6.1)

\cc_{U}=[a_{r}]\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk^{\flat}_{U}}}(\mathbf{Q}_{U_{0}},\mathbf{Q}_{U_{r}}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(a_{r})\rangle}).

Similarly, the relative fundamental class of
a^{\bot}_{r}
defines a cohomological correspondence

(10.6.2)

\cc_{U^{\bot}}=[a^{\bot}_{r}]\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk^{\flat}_{U^{\bot}}}}(\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{0}},\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{r}}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(a^{\bot}_{r})\rangle}).

By
[
FYZ22
, Corollary 9.1.5]
the pushforward of cohomological correspondences (§
4.3.1
) along the morphism of correspondences
f:\Hk^{\flat}_{U}\to\Hk^{\flat}_{V}
is defined, giving

(10.6.3)

f_{!}(\cc_{U})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk^{\flat}_{V}}}(f_{0!}\mathbf{Q}_{U_{0}},f_{r!}\mathbf{Q}_{U_{r}}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(a_{r})\rangle}).

Similarly,

(10.6.4)

f^{\bot}_{!}(\cc_{U^{\bot}})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk^{\flat}_{\widehat{V}}}}(f^{\bot}_{0!}\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{0}},f^{\bot}_{r!}\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{r}}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(a^{\bot}_{r})\rangle})

is defined.

For a
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant cohomological correspondence
\cc
on a derived vector bundle
E
, we denote by
{}^{\dagger}{\cc}
the descended cohomological correspondence on
{}^{\dagger}{E}
. Recall the notion of homogeneous Fourier transform of cohomological correspondences from §
9.4.1
. We have (simplifying some dimensions as in
[
FYZ22
, §9.4]
)

(10.6.5)

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\Hk^{\flat}_{V}}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}({}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U}}))\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk^{\flat}_{\widehat{V}}}}(\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{V_{0}}(f_{0!}\mathbf{Q}_{U_{0}}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{V_{r}}(f_{r!}\mathbf{Q}_{U_{r}}){\scriptstyle\langle d(\widetilde{b}_{r})-d(a_{r})\rangle}).

Since
U^{\bot}
is the orthogonal complement of
U
relative to
V
(in the derived sense), by §
8.2.7
we have canonical isomorphisms for
i=0,r
:

\displaystyle\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{V_{i}}(f_{i!}\mathbf{Q}_{U_{i}})\cong(f^{\bot}_{i})_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{i}}{\scriptstyle[\rank(\mathcal{V})]}{\scriptstyle\langle-\rank(\mathcal{U})\rangle}.

Note the shift and twist on the right side is the same for
i=0
and
i=r
. Therefore we may view (simplifying some dimensions as in
[
FYZ22
, §9.4]
)

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\Hk^{\flat}_{V}}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}({}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U}}))\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk^{\flat}_{\widehat{V}}}}((f^{\bot}_{0})_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{0}},(f^{\bot}_{r})_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{r}}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(a_{r}^{\bot})\rangle}).

Theorem 10.6.1
.

Recall the shift and twist notation from §
6.5
. Let
\pi_{i}\colon U_{i}\rightarrow S
be the bundle projection. Then we have

\mathbb{T}_{[d(f_{0})+d(\pi_{0})](d(\pi_{0}))}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk_{V}^{\flat}}}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}({}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U}}))=({}^{\dagger}{f^{\perp}})_{!}({}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U^{\perp}}})\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk_{\widehat{V}}^{\flat}}}((f_{0}^{\perp})_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{0}},(f_{r}^{\perp})_{!}\mathbf{Q}_{U^{\bot}_{r}}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(a_{r}^{\perp})\rangle}).

Proof.

Let
\mathfrak{s}\in\mathrm{Corr}_{{}^{\dagger}{\Hk^{r}_{S}}}(\mathbf{Q}_{S},\mathbf{Q}_{S}{\scriptstyle\langle-d(h_{r})\rangle})
be the cohomological correspondence obtained from the relative fundamental class of
h_{r}
as in §
6.3
. Let

\pi:\Hk^{\flat}_{U}\to\Hk_{S}^{r},\quad\pi^{\bot}:\Hk^{\flat}_{U^{\bot}}\to\Hk_{S}^{r},

be the bundle projections viewed as maps of correspondences, and also recall the maps of correspondences

\displaystyle z^{\perp}:\Hk^{r}_{S}\to\Hk^{\flat}_{W^{\perp}},\quad g^{\perp}:\Hk_{\widehat{V}}^{\flat}\to\Hk_{W^{\perp}}^{\flat}.

The proof is completed by the sequence of equalities of cohomological correspondences

\displaystyle\mathbb{T}_{[d(f_{0})+d(\pi_{0})](d(\pi_{0}))}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U}})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(1)}}{{=}}\mathbb{T}_{[d(f_{0})+d(\pi_{0})](d(\pi_{0}))}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{\pi}^{*}{}^{\dagger}{\mathfrak{s}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(2)}}{{=}}({}^{\dagger}{g^{\perp}})^{*}({}^{\dagger}{z^{\perp}})_{!}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}(\mathfrak{s})

\displaystyle\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(3)}}{{=}}({}^{\dagger}{g^{\perp}})^{*}({}^{\dagger}{z^{\perp}})_{!}\mathfrak{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(4)}}{{=}}({}^{\dagger}{f^{\perp}})_{!}({}^{\dagger}{\pi^{\perp}})^{*}\mathfrak{s}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(5)}}{{=}}({}^{\dagger}{f^{\perp}})_{!}{}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U^{\perp}}}.

Here:

(1), (5)

follow from the
\mathbf{G}_{m}
-equivariant identifications

\pi^{*}\mathfrak{s}=\cc_{U},\quad(\pi^{\bot})^{*}\mathfrak{s}=\cc_{U^{\bot}}

which are proved exactly as in
[
FYZ22
, Lemma 9.4.2]
.

(2)

involves two applications of Proposition
9.4.1
, namely

\mathbb{T}_{[d(f_{0})]}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}\circ{}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}=({}^{\dagger}{g}^{\perp})^{*}\circ\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}},\quad\mathbb{T}_{[d(\pi_{0})](d(\pi_{0}))}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}\circ{}^{\dagger}{\pi}^{*}={}^{\dagger}{z}^{\perp}_{!}\circ\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}.

(3)

is the trivial equality
\mathfrak{s}=\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\Hk_{S}^{r}}(\mathfrak{s})
, where
\Hk_{S}^{r}
is regarded as a trivial vector bundle over itself.

(4)

follows from Theorem
4.4.2
. (Note that the hypotheses of Theorem
4.4.2
hold in this situation by
[
FYZ22
, Corollary 9.1.5]
.)

∎

10.7.
Calculations with the homogeneous arithmetic Fourier transform

We denote

(10.7.1)

\displaystyle X^{\circ}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle X-\nu(|Q|\cup|\mathcal{T}_{1}|\cup|\mathcal{T}_{2}|)=X-\nu(|\widetilde{Q}_{1}|)=X-\nu(|\widetilde{Q}_{2}|);

(10.7.2)

\displaystyle X^{\prime\circ}

\displaystyle\coloneqq

\displaystyle\nu^{-1}(X^{\circ}).

For a stack
A
over
X^{\circ}
, we denote

(10.7.3)

A^{\circ}\coloneqq A\times_{X^{r}}(X^{\circ})^{r}.

In particular,
\Hk_{S}^{r,\circ}\subset\Hk_{S}^{r}
denotes the open substack where the legs are all disjoint from
|\widetilde{Q}_{1}|\cup|\widetilde{Q}_{2}|
.

By
[
FYZ22
, Lemma 10.13]
, for each
i
the restriction
\widetilde{b}_{i}^{\circ}\colon\Hk_{V}^{\flat,\circ}\rightarrow\widetilde{V}^{\circ}_{i}
of
\widetilde{b}_{i}
and the restriction
\widetilde{b}^{\prime\circ}_{i}\colon\widetilde{V}^{\circ}_{i}\to\Hk_{V}^{\sharp,\circ}
of
\widetilde{b}^{\prime}_{i}
are isomorphisms. This implies
[
FYZ22
, Corollary 10.1.4]
that the projection map
\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V}\to\Sht^{r,\circ}_{S}=\Sht^{r,\leqslant\mu,\circ}_{U(n)}
is an étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle. Hence the theory of the homogeneous arithmetic Fourier transform (§
9.5
) applies to it.

10.7.1.
Virtual fundamental classes

By
[
FYZ22
, Remark 9.1.1]
the spaces
U_{i}
from §
10.5
can be viewed as the derived fiber of the derived Hitchin stack
\mathscr{M}_{H_{1},H_{2}}
from
[
FYZ21b
, §5]
over
\{\mathcal{E}_{1}\}\times S\rightarrow\Bun_{\GL(m)^{\prime}}\times\Bun_{U(n)}
, where
H_{1}=\GL(m)^{\prime}
and
H_{2}=U(n)
. Similarly, by
[
FYZ22
, Remark 9.1.2]

\Hk_{U}^{\flat}
is an open substack of the derived fiber of the derived Hecke stack
\Hk_{\mathscr{M}_{H_{1},H_{2}}}^{r}
from
[
FYZ21b
, §5]
over
\{\mathcal{E}_{1}\}\times S\rightarrow\Bun_{\GL(m)^{\prime}}\times\Bun_{U(n)}
. Therefore, the derived fibered product

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sht_{U}^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{U}^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{0}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{0}\times U_{r}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a_{0},a_{r})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Id,\Frob)}

is equipped with an open embedding in
\Sht_{\mathscr{M}_{H_{1},H_{2}}}^{r}
, and in particular is of virtual dimension
d(a_{r})
. We then have two natural cycles in
\CH_{d(a_{r})}(\Sht_{U}^{r})
:

(a)

The intrinsic derived fundamental class
[\Sht_{U}^{r}]\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}(\Sht_{U}^{r})
.

(b)

The trace of the cohomological correspondence
\cc_{U}
(calculated using the canonical Weil structure), denoted
\Tr^{\Sht}(\cc_{U})\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}(\Sht_{U}^{r})
(cf. §
6.4.2
).

We assemble the earlier results to calculate the trace of our cohomological correspondences. The assumptions (
10.4.3
) imply that the maps
U_{i}\rightarrow S
and
U_{i}^{\perp}\rightarrow S
are smooth, hence
U_{i}
and
U_{i}^{\perp}
are smooth. Then by Corollary
6.4.1
we have

(10.7.4)

\Tr^{\Sht}(\cc_{U})=[\Sht_{U}^{r}]\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}(\Sht_{U}^{r}).

In particular,
\Sht_{U}^{r}
is an open substack of
\Sht_{\mathcal{M}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}}^{r}
, so it is quasi-smooth and
[\Sht_{U}^{r}]
is the restriction of what was called
[\mathcal{Z}_{\mathcal{E}_{1}}^{r}]
in
[
FYZ21b
]
.

Similarly, we have

(10.7.5)

\Tr^{\Sht}(\cc_{U^{\perp}})=[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r}]\in\CH_{d(a_{r}^{\perp})}(\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r}),

where
\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r}
is defined by the derived Cartesian square

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\Hk_{U^{\perp}}^{\flat}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{0}^{\perp}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces U_{0}^{\perp}\times U_{r}^{\perp}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(a_{0}^{\perp},a_{r}^{\perp})}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\Id,\Frob)}

Next, the assumptions (
10.4.4
) imply that the maps
f_{i}\colon U_{i}\rightarrow V_{i}
,
f\colon\Hk_{U}^{\flat}\rightarrow\Hk_{V}^{\flat}
,
f_{i}^{\perp}\colon U_{i}^{\perp}\rightarrow\widehat{V}_{i}
, and
f^{\perp}\colon\Hk_{U^{\perp}}^{\flat}\rightarrow\Hk_{\widehat{V}}^{\flat}
are all closed embeddings. Then Proposition
6.2.1
applies to give

(10.7.6)

\Tr^{\Sht}(f_{!}\cc_{U})=\Sht(f)_{!}\Tr^{\Sht}(\cc_{U})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq: trace of cc_U})}}{{=}}\Sht(f)_{!}[\Sht_{U}^{r}]\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}(\Sht_{V}^{r}),

where we write
\Sht(f)\coloneqq\Fix(f^{(1)})\colon\Sht_{U}^{r}\rightarrow\Sht_{V}^{r}
for the map induced by taking fixed points of the twisted cohomological correspondence
\mathfrak{\cc}_{U}^{(1)}
, and similarly for other cohomological correspondences. We similarly have

(10.7.7)

\Tr^{\Sht}(f_{!}^{\perp}\cc_{U^{\perp}})=\Sht(f^{\perp})_{!}\Tr^{\Sht}(\cc_{U^{\perp}})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle(\ref{eq: trace of cc_U perp})}}{{=}}\Sht(f^{\perp})_{!}[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r}]\in\CH_{d(a_{r}^{\perp})}(\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r}).

Notation 10.7.1
.

For an
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space stack
Y\rightarrow T
as in §
9.5.1
and a class
\alpha\in\CH_{*}(Y)
(resp.
\CH^{*}(Y)
), we denote

{}^{\dagger}{\alpha}=\Av(\alpha)\coloneqq\frac{1}{q-1}\pr_{!}(\alpha)\in\CH_{*}({}^{\dagger}{Y})\quad\text{ (resp. $\CH^{*}({}^{\dagger}{Y})$)}

where
\pr\colon Y\rightarrow{}^{\dagger}{Y}
is the quotient map.

Example 10.7.2

(Homogeneous cycles)
.

We say that
\alpha
on
Y
is
homogeneous
if
\alpha=\pr^{*}({}^{\dagger}{\alpha})
. Note that
[\Sht^{r}_{U}]\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}(\Sht_{U}^{r})
is homogeneous, hence
\Sht(f)_{!}[\Sht^{r}_{U}]\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}(\Sht_{V}^{r})
is homogeneous.

10.7.2.
Arithmetic Fourier transform of cycles

Recall that
\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V}\to\Sht^{r,\circ}_{S}
is an étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle. We now relate the cycle classes (
10.7.6
) and (
10.7.7
) under the homogeneous arithmetic Fourier transform on
\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V}
as defined in §
9.5
.

Theorem 10.7.3
.

We have

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}(\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}({}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}]}))=(-1)^{d(U/S)+d(f_{0})}q^{d(U/S)}\cdot\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}_{!}({}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r,\circ}]})\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}({}^{\dagger}{\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r,\circ}}).

Here
\Sht(f)^{\circ}:\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}\to\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V}
is the restriction of
\Sht(f)
, and similarly for
\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}
. We use the same notation for induced maps on the homogeneous quotients
{}^{\dagger}{(-)}
. (We are using
[
FYZ22
, Remark 10.2.2]
to match the degrees of homology.)

Proof.

We apply Theorem
9.6.1
with
E=V
,
C_{E}=\Hk_{V}^{\flat,\circ}
and
\cc=(f_{!}\cc_{U})|_{\Hk_{V}^{\flat,\circ}}
. Then Theorem
9.6.1
tells us that

(10.7.8)

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}_{\Sht_{V}^{r,\circ}}\left(\Tr^{\Sht}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U}})|_{{}^{\dagger}{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V}}}\right)=\left(\Tr^{\Sht}\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\Hk_{V}^{\flat}}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U}})\right)|_{{}^{\dagger}{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{\widehat{V}}}}\in\CH_{d(a_{r})}({}^{\dagger}{\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r,\circ}}).

By Theorem
10.6.1
we have

\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{ren}}_{\Hk_{V}^{\flat}}({}^{\dagger}{f}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U}})=\mathbb{T}_{[-d(U/S)-d(f_{0})](-d(U/S))}({}^{\dagger}{f}^{\perp})_{!}{}^{\dagger}{\cc_{U^{\perp}}}.

Putting this into (
10.7.8
) and then taking the trace, using (
6.5.1
), (
10.7.6
) and (
10.7.7
), yields the result.
∎

10.7.3.
Test functions

We introduce some notation for functions on
\Sht_{V}
and
\Sht_{\widehat{V}}
. The decompositions
\widetilde{Q}_{1}\coloneqq Q^{*}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{1}^{*}\oplus\sigma^{*}\mathcal{T}_{2}
and
\widetilde{Q}_{2}\coloneqq\sigma^{*}Q\oplus\sigma^{*}\mathcal{T}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{T}_{2}^{*}
induce the following decompositions defined in
[
FYZ22
, §10.2.1]
(with the same notation):

\Sht_{V}^{r}=\Sht_{V^{(0)}}^{r}\times_{\Sht_{S}^{r}}\Sht_{V^{(1)}}^{r}\times_{\Sht_{S}^{r}}\Sht_{V^{(2)}}^{r},

\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r}=\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(0)}}^{r}\times_{\Sht_{S}^{r}}\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(1)}}^{r}\times_{\Sht_{S}^{r}}\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(2)}}^{r}.

We note that
\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}
is dual to
\Sht_{V^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}
as
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector spaces over
\Sht^{r,\circ}_{S}
in the sense of §
9.5.1
.

We denote
\mathfrak{q}_{12}\colon\Sht^{r}_{V^{(0)}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbf{F}_{q}}
and
\mathfrak{q}_{21}\colon\Sht^{r}_{V^{(0)}}\rightarrow\underline{\mathbf{F}_{q}}
the two quadratic forms induced by the Hermitian structures
h_{12}
and
h_{21}
on
Q
from
[
FYZ22
, §2.3.3]
, respectively. Namely,
\mathfrak{q}_{12}
is the composition

(10.7.9)

\mathfrak{q}_{12}:\Sht^{r}_{V^{(0)}}\xrightarrow{(\Id,h_{12})}\Sht^{r}_{V^{(0)}}\times_{\Sht^{r}_{S}}\Sht^{r}_{\widehat{V}^{(0)}}\to\underline{\mathbf{F}_{q}},

and similarly for
\mathfrak{q}_{21}
. They are related by
\mathfrak{q}_{12}=-\mathfrak{q}_{21}
. Recall the additive character
\psi\colon\mathbf{F}_{q}\rightarrow\overline{\mathbf{Q}}^{\times}
from §
10.2
.

•

We let
\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}\psi
be the pullback of
\psi
to
\Sht_{V^{(0)}}^{r}
via
\mathfrak{q}_{12}
, and similarly for
\mathfrak{q}_{21}
. Abusing notation, we will also use the same notation
\mathfrak{q}^{*}_{12}\psi
to denote its pullback to
\Sht_{V}^{r}
and to
\Sht^{r,\circ}_{\widehat{V}}
. The meaning will be clear from context.

•

We let
\delta_{\Sht_{V^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}}
be the indicator function of the zero-section of the étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle
\Sht_{V^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}\rightarrow\Sht_{S}^{r,\circ}
. Abusing notation, we will also use this same notation to denote its pullback to
\Sht_{V}^{r,\circ}
.

•

We let
\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{V^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}}
be the constant function of
\Sht_{V^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}
with value
1
. Abusing notation, we will also use this same notation to denote its pullback to
\Sht_{V}^{r,\circ}
.

•

We use similar notation on
\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r,\circ}
and
{}^{\dagger}{\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r,\circ}}
.

Lemma 10.7.4
.

Let
d^{(i)}
be the rank of
\Sht_{V^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}
as an étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle over
\Sht_{S}^{r,\circ}
. Let
d=d^{(0)}+d^{(1)}+d^{(2)}
be the rank of
\Sht_{V}^{r,\circ}
as an étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle over
\Sht_{S}^{r,\circ}
. Then we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}_{\Sht_{V}^{r,\circ}}\left(\Av({(\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}\psi)}\cdot{\delta_{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V^{(1)}}}}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V^{(2)}}}})\right)=(-1)^{d}q^{d^{(2)}+\frac{1}{2}d^{(0)}}\eta_{F^{\prime}/F}(D_{Q})^{n}\cdot\Av((\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}[-1]^{*}\psi)\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V^{(1)}}}\cdot\delta_{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{V^{(2)}}})

as functions on
{}^{\dagger}{\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r,\circ}}
.

Proof.

This follows from
[
FYZ22
, Corollary 10.2.4]
by applying
\Av(-)
.
∎

Lemma 10.7.5
.

For
i=1,2
, let
\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}}
be the Hitchin base as in
[
FYZ21b
, §3.3]
. For
a\in\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}}(k)
, recall that
\mathcal{Z}^{r,\leq\mu,\circ}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}_{i}}}(a)\coloneqq\mathcal{Z}^{r}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{i}}(a)\times_{\Sht^{r}_{U(n)}}\Sht^{r,\circ}_{S}
.

(1) We have an equality in
\CH_{d(a_{r})}({}^{\dagger}{\Sht_{V}^{r,\circ}})
:

(\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}]})\cdot\Av({(\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}\psi)}\cdot{\delta_{\Sht_{V^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{V^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}})=\Av\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}}(k)}\psi(\langle e_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E}_{1}},a\rangle)\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}[\mathcal{Z}^{r,\leq\mu,\circ}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}}(a)]\right).

(2) We have an equality in
\CH_{d(a_{r})}({}^{\dagger}{\Sht_{\widehat{V}}^{r,\circ}})
:

(\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r,\circ}]})\cdot\Av({(\mathfrak{q}_{21}^{*}\psi)}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}}\cdot{\delta_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}})=\Av\left(\sum_{a\in\mathcal{A}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}(k)}\psi(\langle e_{\mathcal{G},\mathcal{E}_{2}},a\rangle)\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}_{!}[\mathcal{Z}^{r,\leq\mu,\circ}_{\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}}(a)]\right).

Proof.

This follows from
[
FYZ22
, Lemma 10.2.7]
by applying
\Av(-)
.
∎

10.8.
Proof of Theorem
10.3.1

We will now complete the proof of Theorem
10.3.1
. Let
d,d^{(i)}
, for
i\in\{0,1,2\}
, be be the rank of
\Sht_{V^{(i)}}^{r,\circ}
as an étale
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space bundle over
\Sht_{S}^{r,\circ}
. Note that
d^{(i)}
is also the rank of
V^{(i)}
as a vector bundle over
S
.

From
[
FYZ22
, (10.3.1)]
we may rewrite the higher theta series in the following way, using the non-homogeneous variant of Notation
9.5.1
,

(10.8.1)

\widetilde{Z}_{m}^{r}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1},\mathcal{G})|_{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{S}}=\chi(\det\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1})q^{n(\deg\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}-\deg\omega_{X})/2}\langle\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}[\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}],\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}\psi\cdot\delta_{\Sht_{V^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{V^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}\rangle

and

(10.8.2)

\widetilde{Z}_{m}^{r}(\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2},\mathcal{G})|_{\Sht^{r,\circ}_{S}}=\chi(\det\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2})q^{n(\deg\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}-\deg\omega_{X})/2}\langle\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}_{!}[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r,\circ}],\mathfrak{q}_{21}^{*}\psi\cdot\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}\cdot\delta_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}\rangle.

Since
\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}[\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}]
and
\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}_{!}[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r,\circ}]
are homogeneous (cf. Example
10.7.2
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\chi(\det\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1})q^{n(\deg\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{1}-\deg\omega_{X})/2}\langle\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}\ {}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}]},\Av(\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}\psi\cdot{\delta_{\Sht_{V^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{V^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}})\rangle

(10.8.3)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\chi(\det\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2})q^{n(\deg\widetilde{\mathcal{E}}_{2}-\deg\omega_{X})/2}\langle\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}_{!}\ {}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r,\circ}]},\Av(\mathfrak{q}_{21}^{*}\psi\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}}\cdot{\delta_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}})\rangle.

Let
d=d^{(0)}+d^{(1)}+d^{(2)}
be the rank of
\Sht_{V}^{r,\circ}
as an
\mathbf{F}_{q}
-vector space over
\Sht_{S}^{r,\circ}
. By the Plancherel formula from Lemma
9.5.2
and the involutivity of
\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}
from Lemma
9.5.3
, we have

(10.8.4)

\displaystyle\langle\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}]},\Av(\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}\psi\cdot{\delta_{\Sht_{V^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{V^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}})\rangle

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q^{d}}\langle\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}(\Sht(f)^{\circ}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U}^{r,\circ}]}),\mathrm{FT}^{\mathrm{arith}}(\Av(\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}\psi\cdot{\delta_{\Sht_{V^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}}\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{V^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}}))\rangle.

Using Theorem
10.7.3
and Lemma
10.7.4
, we rewrite the RHS of (
10.8.4
) as

(10.8.5)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{q^{d}}q^{d(U/S)}(-1)^{d(U/S)+d(f_{0})}(-1)^{d}q^{d^{(2)}+\frac{1}{2}d^{(0)}}\eta_{F^{\prime}/F}(D_{Q})^{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\cdot\langle\Sht(f^{\perp})^{\circ}_{!}{}^{\dagger}{[\Sht_{U^{\perp}}^{r,\circ}]},\Av(\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}[-1]^{*}\psi\cdot{\mathbbm{1}_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(1)}}^{r,\circ}}}\cdot{\delta_{\Sht_{\widehat{V}^{(2)}}^{r,\circ}}})\rangle.

Since
\mathfrak{q}_{12}=-\mathfrak{q}_{21}
, we have
\mathfrak{q}_{12}^{*}[-1]^{*}=\mathfrak{q}_{21}^{*}
. Then clearly (
10.8.5
) agrees with the RHS of (
10.8
) up to sign and exponent of
q
. These signs and the exponents of
q
on either side are matched exactly as in
[
FYZ22
, §10.3]
.
